#5152. Shiba Inu Heads for Recovery as Netflow Turns Red With Over 114 Billion SHIB

Shiba Inu has begun to show signs of a potential recovery after multiple days of severe price correction as the broader crypto market continues to face increasing sell pressure.

While momentum appears to be shifting, the latest on-chain data from the crypto analytics platform CryptoQuant shows that buyers are beginning to dominate the Shiba Inu market once again.

Although Shiba Inu is still struggling amid the prolonged market volatility as its price continues to trade in the red territory, its exchange activity is providing a different signal.

According to the data, the Shiba Inu exchange network has declined modestly by around 1.37% over the last day, sitting at -114,647,400,000 SHIB as of the time of writing.

The negative netflow balance indicates an increase in the demand for the leading meme token among investors as it means that the number of SHIB tokens that have been moved out of exchanges for buy purposes over the last 24 hours has substantially outweighed the number of SHIB tokens that flowed into exchanges in attempts to sell.

Apparently, this means that traders have bought more tokens than they have sold over the last day, positioning Shiba Inu for a bullish price reversal after the sharp price declines seen in the past days.

Although Shiba Inu is still showing mixed price action as it is yet to respond to the bullish exchange activity seen over the last day, market participants are regaining confidence in its potential price move.

While its price movement still remains slow, its hourly price chart shows that momentum is gradually changing as it has turned green, suggesting that Shiba Inu might be on the verge of recovery.

#5151. Don’t Store Crypto on iPhone, Ledger CTO Says

Ledger Chief Technology Officer Charles Guillemet has recently warned cryptocurrency holders not to store critical wallet information on their iPhones, as the security threat is on the rise.

In his recent post on X, the Ledger executive highlighted the fact that vulnerabilities that can leak important data are now becoming more and more accessible for hackers to exploit.

"Unfortunately this kind of vulnerability used to be very expensive and reserved to high potential attackers," Guillemet tweeted.

However, he pointed out that we're now on the verge of seeing the costs of such vulnerabilities go down to zero, meaning that it could become very easy for cybercriminals to exploit them on a massive scale.

"Simply don't store any valuable in your mobile. Especially not your crypto seeds," the Ledger CTO cautioned.

His words came in response to an urgent security alert published by crypto exchange Binance over a vulnerability that was found in Apple's iOS operating system.

In particular, cryptocurrency exchange Binance reported a vulnerability, dubbed worse_query, which can allow malicious applications to gain access to files of other applications installed on the same device.

As specified by the exchange, this security flaw affects iOS 26.7.1, 27.0.1 and 27.2.

This means that crypto wallet recovery phrases, private keys and login credentials can be at risk, as they may be stored in accessible local files.

Binance underscored that this concern isn't exclusive to the exchange's app alone, while adding that the vulnerability can have an impact on the entire crypto space.

The exchange recommended that users uninstall apps that were downloaded from untrusted sources, update their operating systems and avoid installing any unauthorized software.

Binance also advised users to transfer their funds to newly created wallets on clean devices if they think their wallets may have been compromised.

Apple published new security updates on Nov. 9. However, it's not clear if they are enough to fully patch this vulnerability. Still, there have been no confirmed crypto thefts specifically linked to the exploit.

Guillemet's warning comes a month after Ledger's major security incident involving compromised hardware wallets.

Back in October, French cryptocurrency hardware wallet maker Ledger admitted that some hardware wallets distributed by its Southeast Asian reseller CryptoBilis had been tampered with.

It turned out that hackers managed to install complex hardware implants in legitimate Ledger devices. With that, hackers were able to intercept users' recovery phrases when people were setting up their Ledger wallets for the first time.

As shown by Ledger's investigation, malicious implants were capable of capturing the 24-word recovery phrase that appears on the Ledger device screen and then passing it to attackers via a cellular connection.

Blockchain sleuths estimated that this incident might have led to more than $86 million in crypto losses. However, Ledger itself didn't confirm the total amount.

#5150. Trump Media posts $410M profit, but crypto hides an operating loss

Trump Media reported $409.7 million in Q3 net income, driven largely by non-cash items, alongside $496.1 million in digital asset and equity gains.

Trump Media & Technology Group reported $409.7 million in net income for the third quarter of 2026, saying most of it reflected non-cash items, as gains on digital assets and equity securities drove the result.

The company announced the results for the quarter ended Sept. 30 in its Nov. 9 earnings release, furnished with a Form 8-K. It reported combined net realized and unrealized gains on digital assets and pledged digital assets and equity securities of $496.1 million.

Trump Media reported $3 million in quarterly revenue and a $12.3 million loss in adjusted earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation, and amortization (EBITDA).

The release's reconciliation and accompanying quarterly filing separately identify $378.1 million in realized and unrealized gains on digital assets and digital assets pledged, namely Bitcoin and Cronos.

Digital assets and pledged digital assets supplied most of the combined gain pool. Equity-security gains made up the rest; reported net income was $409.7 million.

The $378.1M digital-asset line includes pledged-asset revaluation, other digital-asset revaluation and derecognition gains.

Published unrealized and realized equity gains sum to $118.0M, rounded. The company says gains were driven primarily by bitcoin-related securities.

The $496.1M gain pool combines digital assets and securities. It is distinct from $409.7M in net income.

Realized, unrealized and derecognition gains are accounting categories, not equivalent cash proceeds. The gain pool is not a complete net-income bridge; other income and expenses also enter earnings.

The 10-Q breaks the digital asset line into about $200.8 million in unrealized gains on pledged digital assets, $107.5 million in unrealized gains on digital assets, and $69.9 million from derecognition of digital assets.

Derecognition means removing assets from the balance sheet, so the reported gains should not be read as cash generated during the quarter.

The 10-Q identifies about $98.3 million of unrealized equity gains and $19.7 million of realized equity gains, driven primarily by higher prices of bitcoin-related securities. Some of the investment gains outside the digital asset line also came from Bitcoin-linked exposure.

The release said gains were partially offset by $54 million in realized and unrealized derivative losses. Other highlighted offsets included $11.9 million in accreted interest expense, $9.4 million in stock-based compensation, and $4 million in investment expenses.

Trump Media said revenue rose 205% from a year earlier, reporting rounded figures of $3 million versus $1 million, and the 10-Q provides underlying amounts of approximately $3 million versus $972,900.

The filing attributes the increase to $1.3 million in subscription growth driven by Truth API and Truth+, about $523,300 in additional Truth Social advertising revenue, and $164,000 in Truth.Fi ETF management fees.

Those revenue gains sit alongside an adjusted EBITDA of a $12.3 million loss after adjustments for digital asset gains and losses, investment income and losses, net interest, taxes, depreciation and amortization, and stock-based compensation.

At Sept. 30, the company reported $2.5 billion in total assets and approximately $2.3 billion in what it calls financial assets. The latter is a non-GAAP basket spanning cash, restricted cash, short-term investments, equity securities, notes receivable and accrued interest, digital assets and pledged digital assets.

The company says financial assets should not be treated as a liquidity measure, and the basket is broader than a Bitcoin treasury total.

For the first nine months of 2026, the profitable third quarter still left a consolidated net loss of approximately $234.2 million. Trump Media also said it used $28.4 million in operating activities over the nine months ended Sept. 30, primarily because of expenses tied to recently concluded legacy legal matters.

#5148. Microsoft-Backed Space and Time Launches Platform to Let AI Agents Verify Private Data

Microsoft-Backed Space and Time Launches Platform to Let AI Agents Verify Private Data

Space and Time, a Microsoft-backed blockchain data project, has launched a platform designed to let businesses use private data in AI tools without handing over the underlying records, the company announced on Monday.

Space and Time Enterprise lets companies prove that calculations made on their private data are correct, so that AI applications, auditors and other stakeholders can check the results without accessing the database itself. Launch partners include onchain asset manager Maple Finance, Indonesian vehicle maker and distributor Indomobil, Oracle software consultancy Trenez and AI data network The Data Foundation.

"Businesses want AI agents to execute workflows using that information, but those agents don't need unrestricted access to the records behind every calculation," Nate Holiday, CEO of MakeInfinite Labs, the original developer of Space and Time, told Decrypt.

The stakes are high, he argued. "The potential cost to a business is the overall value of the business," Holiday said. "Giving AI tools unrestricted access to data creates another avenue of exposure."

Holiday said the company is working with customers in industries including construction, engineering and manufacturing that want to rebuild their systems around AI, so that agents, reporting tools and compliance teams can work from the same business records and check the calculations they rely on.

The platform is built on Proof of SQL, Space and Time's patented technology for proving that database queries were computed correctly. It already works with Google Cloud's BigQuery, and businesses can now run it on their own servers without operating a blockchain, buying specialized hardware or relying on an outside proving service, according to the company.

With GPU acceleration, supported queries over millions of rows can be run and proven in under a second, Holiday said, though actual performance depends on the query, hardware and dataset.

The technology proves that a calculation was carried out correctly on a given set of records, but it cannot tell whether those records were accurate to begin with. "An incorrect entry still needs to be caught through the business's data controls," Holiday said, "but verification gives those controls a much stronger foundation: evidence of which data was used and whether it was processed correctly."

Space and Time raised $20 million in 2022 in a strategic funding round led by M12, Microsoft's venture fund. Last year, Grayscale launched a trust giving investors exposure to SXT, the token that powers Space and Time's blockchain. Earlier this year, the company launched Dreamspace, an AI-powered tool built with Microsoft's Azure AI services that lets users create and deploy onchain apps without writing code.

Space and Time also runs SXT Chain, a decentralized network that supplies verifiable data to DeFi, tokenized assets and stablecoins. Asked whether enterprise customers would create demand for SXT, Holiday said businesses could deploy the product in their own environment, and that the network would come into play as their applications "need to connect to onchain markets," with those workloads paid for through Space and Time.

The company's cryptography is also used elsewhere. Microsoft's Nova project includes Blitzar, Space and Time's cryptographic acceleration library, and the team's work on a commitment scheme called Dory contributed to a16z crypto's Jolt proof system, Holiday said.

#5145. MARA dumps Bitcoin to erase a $750M collateral risk

MARA repaid its Bitcoin-backed loans, but 2027 bond puts, cash resources and spending choices shape the case for further sales.

Bitcoin miner MARA Holdings has repaid its $750 million of Bitcoin-backed loans, ending the collateral-liquidation exposure attached to those facilities.

Further coin sales could now serve to build cash for bondholder demands, AI infrastructure, and possible share buybacks.

The company's Nov. 9 filing disclosed that it sold 13,597 Bitcoin after Sept. 30 for approximately $1.1 billion and used $750 million to repay the loans in full.

Management's quarterly letter says it intends to use Bitcoin to build cash for AI infrastructure opportunities, convertible-note puts exercisable next year and potential repurchases of its own shares.

As of Sept. 30, MARA had $291.6 million of June 2031 notes carrying a June 4, 2027 cash-put right, and $632.5 million of March 2030 notes carrying a December 1, 2027 right. Together, those September balances total about $924.1 million.

A cash put lets a bondholder require the company to buy back all or part of the notes for cash at face value, plus any applicable unpaid special interest.

The notes' contractual maturity dates remain in 2030 and 2031, and the eventual cash bill depends on which holders exercise and how much principal remains outstanding.

A separate obligation of $48 million of notes outstanding at Sept. 30 matures on Dec. 1. The put calendar does not require MARA to sell Bitcoin to meet potential cash demands.

The 2027 holder-put calendar survives the repayment of MARA's Bitcoin-backed loans. Explore how the potential route to selling changed.

The outstanding Coinbase and Two Prime loans exposed pledged Bitcoin to conditional lender liquidation remedies.

The Nov. 9 disclosures report full repayment after quarter-end. Management still identifies cash uses for continued monetization.

Cash puts are elective holder rights. Repayment removes one liquidation channel while management still plans Bitcoin monetization.

Principal only; applicable accrued unpaid special interest is additional. Payments depend on holder exercise and balances at the dates. Loan payoff occurred after Sept. 30, disclosed Nov. 9; exact transaction dates were not supplied. No current cash balance or inevitable future Bitcoin sale is implied.

#5144. Upbit lists HYPE – Can Hyperliquid narrow its 45x Spot trading gap?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Hyperliquid has received another major boost, but the impact could be bigger than it initially appears.

Upbit has officially listed HYPE on their South Korean exchange. This is significant, as it means the largest crypto spot exchange in South Korea now holds HYPE. This gives traders in South Korea a massive new pool of liquidity. However, it’s Upbit’s dominance in South Korea that makes the listing even more significant.

According to FP Research, Upbit controlled a significant 68.6% of trading volume across the country’s top five major crypto exchanges in April 2026, much higher than Bithumb’s 19.5%. This gives HYPE access to South Korea’s largest crypto trading platform and a much wider pool of local investors.

Notably, though, Hyperliquid’s [HYPE] expansion into South Korea could be just the beginning.

Recently, Bitso, one of Latin America’s largest crypto exchanges, also added HYPE to its perpetual market. This is interesting since Upbit has a spot listing for HYPE. Put simply, Bitso increases HYPE’s exposure in derivatives, while Upbit provides increased exposure for HYPE to South Korean spot traders.

Together, these listings expand HYPE’s exposure into several new markets and trading segments. However, could the Upbit listing offer more value to Hyperliquid? On-chain activity shows that exposure to South Korea’s large spot market may attract some new buying interest and create increased spot liquidity to help reinvigorate the HYPE ecosystem.

Hyperliquid continues to lead the perp trading market, with competitors still struggling to close the gap.

According to DeFiLlama data, Hyperliquid saw more than $65 billion in perp volume this week alone, compared to Aster’s $11 billion. This shows how much of a commanding lead Hyperliquid has in derivatives. Meanwhile, Q4 perp volume has already reached nearly 50% of Q3’s $620 billion total, suggesting that trading activity on Hyperliquid remains strong despite broader market weakness.

Despite this, there is still plenty of growth left to come. Looking at the chart below, Hyperliquid has been averaging around $15 billion each quarter in spot trading volume this year. However, in Q3 2025 it hit $54 billion.

Looking at the gap between this and the derivatives volume in Q3 2025, which hit around $630 billion in perps, it is safe to say that Hyperliquid’s derivatives volume is almost 45x larger than Spot volume. In essence, Hyperliquid still depends heavily on derivatives activity to drive its on-chain momentum.

Against this backdrop, Upbit’s listing could put HYPE in a stronger position to close that gap. The opportunity to access South Korea’s big spot market opens up new buying opportunities, increasing spot liquidity, and helps diversify Hyperliquid’s trading activity beyond perpetual futures.

If this momentum continues, spot growth could become an important driver of the next phase of Hyperliquid’s ecosystem expansion.

Upbit’s 68.6% market share gives HYPE access to South Korea’s largest spot trading market.

With perp volume nearly 45 times larger than spot volume, Upbit’s listing could bring new buyers, boost spot activity and support HYPE’s growth.

Crypto exchange Gemini has unveiled the biggest losers in the altcoin space for 2026 so far, as many of the crypto world's biggest digital currencies have been seeing massive double-digit price plunges.

Crypto exchange Gemini recently shared a list of 10 cryptocurrencies that have seen particularly brutal losses since the start of 2026 in a post on the X social media platform.

Topping Gemini's list of the biggest losers for this year is Bitcoin Cash (BCH), which has crashed a shocking 61.76% year-to-date.

Coming in second is Polkadot (DOT), with a 53.03% price decline, while Cardano (ADA) holds third place after shedding 48.04% of its value.

Ripple-related XRP and the top meme cryptocurrency Dogecoin (DOGE) are next in line, with losses of 43.43% and 42.38%, respectively.

Several other major cryptocurrencies are also seeing considerable losses, such as Sui (SUI), which has fallen 42.08%, Hedera (HBAR), which has dropped 38.13%, Solana (SOL), down 34.02%, Avalanche (AVAX), which has lost 33.40%, and Kaspa (KAS), which has gone down 30.75%.

Declining liquidity in the market, weakening speculative interest, lackluster institutional interest and general macroeconomic conditions are all major factors causing most major altcoins to underperform so far.

But for some, other issues within their ecosystems are also proving troublesome.

While Ripple continues its expansion in the institutional payments sector, stablecoin and tokenization areas, this hasn't necessarily led to sustained buying interest in XRP.

In fact, spot trading volume for XRP fell 53.5%, while perpetual futures trading volume dropped 44%.

Dogecoin, on the other hand, has been contending with waning speculative interest in meme cryptocurrencies.

Its institutional investment narrative also hasn't really caught on yet, with Bitwise announcing in September the shutdown of its Dogecoin ETF as the product failed to draw investor interest.

Bitcoin Cash has been seeing particularly sharp declines as it is now experiencing a reversal of its stellar 2025 performance, when the cryptocurrency soared around 38%.

Selling pressure picked up in May and June 2026 as BCH saw back-to-back monthly drops of over 30%.

A rebound could be seen in September after CME Group announced Bitcoin Cash futures, but this proved insufficient to stop the ongoing downtrend.

Rothschild Investment’s filing, made on Nov. 10, indicates that the company has invested in 6,300 shares of the Bitwise XRP ETF.

The investment in the Bitwise XRP ETF is listed under CUSIP 09174F107 in Rothschild Investment’s third-quarter report.

Interestingly, Rothschild Investment did not have any reported holdings of the XRP ETF in its previous quarterly filing, which indicates that the firm’s investment in the XRP ETF occurred in the third quarter of 2026.

The Bitwise XRP ETF, listed under the ticker XRP, was introduced in November 2025.

The ETF enables traditional investors to invest in the Ripple-linked cryptocurrency without needing to buy and hold XRP tokens themselves.

Although Rothschild Investment has invested in the XRP ETF, it is not a significant part of its overall portfolio.

The firm’s total portfolio value for 13F-reportable holdings is around $2.69 billion.

Rothschild Investment holds much larger stakes in Bitcoin and Ethereum investments.

When it comes to cryptocurrencies, Rothschild Investment’s largest holding is in the iShares Bitcoin Trust ETF by BlackRock, where the company owns 40,188 shares worth around $1.9 million.

Additionally, Rothschild Investment reported holding $911,300 in the Grayscale Bitcoin Trust ETF and $335,160 in the Morgan Stanley Bitcoin Trust ETF.

Other Bitcoin-related investments include the Grayscale Bitcoin Mini Trust ETF ($125,113), VanEck Bitcoin ETF ($118,100), Bitwise Bitcoin ETF ($116,802) and Fidelity Wise Origin Bitcoin Fund ($2,107).

In total, the seven Bitcoin investments represent around $3.51 million in reported holdings.

The company also has a significant holding in the Bitwise Crypto Industry Innovators ETF, with 24,250 shares worth $615,223.

The firm owns $38,360 worth of shares in the Grayscale CoinDesk Crypto 5 ETF.

Finally, it has invested $69,900 in a Solana ETF.

Aside from cryptocurrency investment funds, Rothschild Investment has shares in publicly traded companies that are heavily involved in the digital asset space.

Michael Saylor’s Strategy is one such holding, with a $530,763 investment.

Other holdings include Coinbase Global ($86,681), Block ($125,060) and Riot Platforms ($42,980), a Bitcoin mining company.

#5139. Bitcoin devs merge 3 new BIPs, and transaction fees could plummet

The Bitcoin proposals could cut fees by up to 27% through signature aggregation, with wallet upgrades and network activation still required.

Bitcoin's proposal repository added three draft specifications on Nov. 2-4 that could lower fees for payments assembled jointly by multiple users and for consolidating many inputs into one output.

BIP458 merged on Nov. 2, BIP459 on Nov. 3, and BIP460 on Nov. 4. Their mechanism combines the signatures attached to transaction inputs, the coins being spent.

Half-aggregation combines independently produced signatures without an interactive signing session: n signatures of 64 bytes become 32(n+1) bytes. Full aggregation requires coordinated signing but produces a single 64-byte signature for all aggregated signatures in a group.

For ten signatures, that means shrinking 640 bytes to 352 or 64 bytes, respectively. That's a 45% and 90% reduction in signature payload, before encoding overhead. The reduction in the whole transaction's fee would be much smaller.

Bitcoin fees are commonly expressed per virtual byte (vB). Under Bitcoin's weight rules, virtual size is transaction weight divided by four, rounded up.

Hypothetical examples put all inputs in one eligible aggregation group and use default signing settings, 34-byte output scripts and no optional extra witness data, or annex. Transaction overhead, witness encoding, and the group's marker byte are included.

A joint transaction with ten inputs and ten outputs would shrink from 1,016 vB to 944 vB with half-aggregation or 872 vB with full aggregation, lowering its fee by about 7.1% or 14.2%. At an illustrative 10 satoshis per vB, the savings would be 720 or 1,440 satoshis.

Consolidation benefits more in the second example of 100 inputs combined into one output. Its size falls from 5,804 vB to 5,012 vB with half-aggregation or 4,220 vB with full aggregation, cutting fees by approximately 13.6% or 27.3%.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Ten signatures can shrink by 90% with full aggregation. Compare how much the whole fee could fall in two hypothetical transactions.

10 inputs, 10 outputs. Many outputs leave less of the transaction available for signature compression.

100 inputs, 1 output. Signature-heavy consolidation has a larger percentage fee benefit under these assumptions.

The input/output mix changes the fee benefit. These Draft proposals still require a soft fork and wallet support.

Hypothetical examples: all inputs eligible in one group, default sighashes, no annex, 34-byte output scripts. Blue includes the group marker in transaction panels; top section is signature payload alone. Not current network averages or available discounts. Activation parameters remain unset.

Original calculations from BIP460 witness forms and BIP141/144 encoding. Base=10+41n+43m bytes; W0=4B+2+66n, Wh=4B+2+2n+32(n+1)+1, Wf=4B+2+2n+65. vB=ceil(W/4). Bars scale by weight; labels are rounded vB. Fee savings=(v0-v)/v0 at a fixed rate, rounded to 0.1%.

Hypothetical examples: all inputs eligible in one group, default sighashes, no annex, 34-byte output scripts. Blue includes the group marker in transaction panels; top section is signature payload alone. Not current network averages or available discounts. Activation parameters remain unset.

BIP460 leaves activation parameters unspecified, and its proposed witness version, currently v2, needs coordination with other proposals.

Wallets would need to support the new output type, as existing Taproot outputs would not automatically gain aggregation, which is limited to direct signature-based or key-path spends.

Spends that execute scripts, known as script-path spends, cannot join aggregation groups, and eligible inputs can opt out.

Full aggregation needs a stateful two-round signing protocol, and reusing secret nonces can expose signing keys, making hardware and air-gapped support demanding. Half-aggregation avoids that joint signing session, although collaborative transaction construction still requires agreement.

#5138. Bitcoin nears a ‘thinly traded pocket’ – Is $64K next for BTC?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

The equities volume on Hyperliquid has overtaken the Bitcoin [BTC] volume once again, Glassnode revealed in a post on X.

Crypto-native traders were chasing the stock market dip. The last time the trading volume ratio climbed to such highs was in August, before the bullish BTC rally from $63k to $87k.

It was appealing to consider that another such rebound was around the corner. However, the past three trading days saw Bitcoin spot ETF outflows of $1.043 billion.

Sentiment simply does not support a recovery. At least, not yet.

The STH to LTH supply ratio was above the upper band and headed higher compared to last week. This meant short-term holders accounted for a greater share of the Bitcoin supply.

In turn, it indicated a market more sensitive to short-term price moves, as short-term holder coins tend to carry thin profits and tend to be moved first under stress.

Glassnode’s Market Pulse report also showed a drop in the percentage of supply in profit. The profitable supply was shrinking, but it has not crossed below the lower band.

Therefore, the market was still in a cooling phase, not capitulation. Moreover, fewer holders were sitting on profits that could tempt them to sell, which should ease the profit-taking pressure.

The Coinbase Premium was negative even when the rally began in August. Its -0.135% reading now meant Bitcoin was cheaper on Coinbase than it was on Binance. The discount had been wider toward the end of October as selling pressure ramped up.

This is considered a signal that U.S.-based investor interest in BTC was low.

The net taker buy-sell imbalance was at -13,142 BTC in the futures market on the 9th of November, reported crypto analyst Axel Adler Jr. The futures imbalance had been at -75,000 on the 5th of November and has contracted since then.

Even so, the overall market regime has shifted away from the bullish signals it displayed a few short weeks ago. Bitcoin was above the 200-day Moving Average at $73,193, but not by much.

The imminent threat was the $64k-$74k pocket. In August, BTC prices had ripped through this area, making it a thinly traded pocket. It could offer little resistance on the way down, too.

Chainlink sees a death cross approaching on the chart, with a solid possibility of an aggravation of the current state of affairs on the market. LINK is trading at around $10.49 at the moment, moving below every moving average on the daily chart.

EMAs are also converging – an important sign that might cause some worrying certainly. The longest moving average on the chart, at around $11, was lost in early November, and LINK hasn't been able to get back above it since.

For now, the closest support is the $10 price threshold, where buyers already stepped in twice over the last two weeks. If the death cross gets confirmed and $10 breaks, the drop most likely won't stop there, unfortunately.

Cardano continues the prolonged downtrend, with a possibility of plunging even lower in the foreseeable future. ADA is trading at around $0.1776 at the moment, and it has already lost roughly 37% since the early October peak near $0.28. This week, the price dipped to around $0.1715, back to the levels last seen in mid-August, right before the late-summer rally.

Trend is strong, it's descending, the bear market is certainly having an upper hand here.

RSI is at the bottom though, so there isn't much room for bears to operate here. The indicator is moving below 40, close to the oversold zone, which might slow the selling down for a while.

A strong uptick in volume here would be beneficial. Daily volume in November is among the lowest on the chart, so buyers aren't really showing up even at these prices.

The lack of catalyst is the larger problem: ADA has no traction around it and there's certainly a chance that it won't appear in the foreseeable future.

Fundamentally, ADA had no breakthroughs recently apart from the foundation's support of Cosmos IBC transition to Linux Foundation governance. It's more of an interoperability and institutional infrastructure related development.

If the selling continues, the July range around $0.16 is the next area where ADA could find support. Until volume returns, bracing yourself for more downside might be the wise choice, unfortunately.

Ethereum is certainly struggling, with a solid risk of losing $2,000 price level much sooner than anticipated. Price is already spilling, and somewhat in the freefall. ETH is trading at around $2,095 at the moment, after a big red candle on November 8 took the price down from the $2,230 area.

The asset has already lost roughly 25% since the September peak near $2,800. The current price is also the lowest close since the August breakout, when ETH jumped from the $1,900 area in a matter of days. Most of the late-summer gains are essentially gone.

ETH moves below all major moving averages after losing the black one at around $2,300 in late October. That level is most likely acting as resistance now. The short-term green average is aiming downwards and has already slipped below it, hinting at the aggravation of the current trend.

The November 4 bounce towards $2,450 didn't last either. ETH lost the whole move the very next day, which is an unfortunate sign for anyone hoping for a quick recovery.

RSI is still at the bottom and there's a good chance a bounce is still possible. The asset is clearly oversold. The indicator is hovering close to 30, so sellers might run out of steam soon. If the bounce happens, the black moving average around $2,300 should be the first target.

Volume is descending and overall trend is losing power. Even the November 8 drop came with lower volume than the sell-off at the end of October, so the freefall isn't backed by panic just yet. A strong uptick in selling volume would change the picture, though.

Realistically, the $2,000 price threshold is the first thing buyers have to defend. If it breaks, the next area to watch is around $1,900, where ETH spent most of July and August. A proper recovery, on the other hand, would only start after ETH gets back above $2,230, and that's not happening yet, unfortunately.

DOGE moves below all major moving averages, and all of them are aiming downwards. The closest one sits at around $0.0765, most likely acting as the first resistance in case of a bounce.

RSI is closer to the 'oversold' zone and market's reaction to it isn't yet clear. The indicator is moving around 35 right now. Combination of all of those factors suggests rather calmer price movement, than a severe downtrend that can damage Dogecoin's recovery pathing

Such updates are essentially technical, so they most likely won't move the price on their own. Still, it's a good sign that development around Dogecoin keeps going even when the market isn't favoring the asset.

#5136. NYSE Taps Avalanche to Settle Its Planned Tokenized Stock Platform

NYSE Taps Avalanche to Settle Its Planned Tokenized Stock Platform

The New York Stock Exchange is turning to Avalanche to power the behind-the-scenes plumbing of its forthcoming tokenized securities platform, one of the clearest signs yet that Wall Street’s oldest institutions are moving core market infrastructure onto public blockchains.

Ava Labs, the company behind Avalanche, said Monday that the blockchain will serve as the “core ledger technology” for the clearing firm behind an NYSE-affiliated digital trading venue.

The platform, which the NYSE’s parent Intercontinental Exchange previously said it was developing, is designed to allow 24/7 trading, clearing and settlement of tokenized securities, subject to regulatory approval. Running on Avalanche, it aims to bring the clearing of tokenized equities onchain at institutional scale.

Tokenization, the practice of issuing traditional assets as blockchain-based tokens, has become one of Wall Street’s buzziest pursuits, but most efforts so far have centered on individual products rather than the market machinery itself.

The NYSE’s move is notable because it puts a blockchain underneath the clearing and settlement layer, the plumbing that finalizes trades, rather than just wrapping a single fund or stock in a token.

Dennis Lee, the NYSE’s head of digital issuance, said the exchange is “picking the right technology for each job,” and that while its planned platform will support equities listed across a range of blockchains, the ledger recording and settling trades needs to be fast, reliable and able to control who participates.

Emin Gün Sirer, Ava Labs’ CEO and founder, framed the deal as proof that blockchains can support not just products but the infrastructure they trade on, citing Avalanche’s “24/7 atomic settlement” and predictable costs.

The announcement adds to a frenetic race to put stocks and their underlying infrastructure onchain.

The NYSE separately tapped Blockchain.com to reach crypto investors with tokenized stocks and ETFs, while ICE and crypto exchange OKX are planning a 24/7 tokenized stock venue under an SEC exemption, and Morgan Stanley has moved to tokenize securities.

That OKX-ICE effort has hit friction, though: Every Magnificent Seven stock, including Apple and Meta, opted out of having its shares tokenized on the venue, with 67 companies in all joining them.

Research shows the appetite for tokenized stocks is real but uneven. An IMF study found tokenized stocks show genuine demand yet remain volatile and illiquid, a reminder that the infrastructure may be arriving faster than the markets to fill it.

#5135. Solana perp volume explodes 65% amid shrinking spot activity

GMTrade accounted for 77% of Solana’s tracked weekly perpetuals volume, as trading incentives raise questions about sustained demand.

Solana’s jump in tracked perpetuals trading is concentrated at GMTrade, a derivatives venue that accounts for about 77% of reported weekly turnover in DefiLlama’s Solana table.

Spot trading moved the other way, leaving a narrower demand picture than the derivatives headline suggests.

In the Nov. 10 snapshot, the Solana dashboard reports nearly $9 billion in seven-day perpetuals turnover, up 65.29% from the preceding week. GMTrade, listed by DefiLlama as GMX Solana, contributed nearly $7 billion, with a displayed weekly increase of 105%.

Over the same dashboard’s seven-day comparisons, spot decentralized exchange turnover fell 5% to $9.9 billion, and stablecoin market capitalization fell 4.38% to $14.5 billion.

Dividing GMTrade’s reported weekly turnover by the table’s total gives an approximately 77% share. The rounded totals and growth rates also imply that GMTrade’s weekly increase was roughly as large as the entire table’s gain.

GMTrade’s trading documentation allows SOL exposure backed by either wrapped SOL or USDC collateral. A dollar of leveraged turnover cannot simply be counted as a dollar of new SOL buying.

GMTrade’s GT documentation describes points earned through trading, liquidity provision and referrals, with conditional treasury buybacks offering a route to USDC.

The announced Season 2 terms tie a referrer’s sellable quota to GT earned through referred traders’ trading, so earning points is different from receiving an unrestricted cash rebate.

The published fee documentation lists basic crypto-position opening or closing charges of 0.02% or 0.024%, depending on whether a trade improves the balance of longs and shorts. The Season 2 announcement also described a fee-structure change.

The incentives don't show how much of the weekly increase they caused, or how much trading would persist without them.

GMTrade’s connection to GMX extends beyond the label. GM Labs said in a Sept. 16 governance post that GMX Solana had been integrated into GMX, but GT points remain distinct from the GMX token.

In a Nov. 3 forum reply, contributor Jonezee said Solana-side buybacks were adding GMX to the staking rewards pool.

The Nov. 10 perps table shows nearly $174.9 million in GMTrade open interest, against $235.1 million for the table overall.

Open interest measures outstanding positions, while turnover measures the notional value traded during a period.

A weekly trend requires comparable observations over time, while stablecoin market capitalization measures the price-valued circulating balance. Falling spot turnover likewise measures activity.

The next test is whether GMTrade sustains its turnover across subsequent weeks, with open interest observed over matching periods. That would help distinguish repeat activity from a single weekly jump while the broader spot market remains quieter.

#5134. Hyperscale Data (GPUS) Sells Its Last Bitcoin for AI Data Center, Months After Chasing $100 Million Treasury

Hyperscale Data sold its remaining 211.56 bitcoin for about $16 million by Nov. 8, bringing sales since late July to about 1,113 BTC as it races to energize 10 MW of AI capacity in Michigan in December.

Hyperscale Data has sold the last of its bitcoin, the NYSE American-listed company said Tuesday, unloading about 211.56 BTC for roughly $16 million to pay down debt and fund an AI data center in Michigan.

The sales were completed by Nov. 8 at about $76,000 per coin. Since it first sold part of its reserve in late July, Hyperscale has sold approximately 1,113 bitcoin for about $74.4 million, the company said.

That works out to an average of roughly $66,800 per coin. At today’s price near $74,200, the same 1,113 BTC would be worth about $82.6 million.

The company, which trades under the ticker GPUS, said the money will go toward its Michigan site, where it aims to energize the first 10 megawatts of critical IT capacity in December.

“We believe bringing that capacity online is our quickest path to profitability,” CEO Will Horne said in the release. “Selling our Bitcoin reserves reduces our exposure to Bitcoin price swings and gives us cash to pay down debt and fund the work in Michigan. We believe that’s the best use of this capital for all our stockholders.”

The exit is a sharp reversal from Hyperscale’s plans this summer. In late July, the company reported holding more than 1,106 bitcoin and said it wanted to build a $100 million digital asset treasury and reach full parity between its bitcoin holdings and its market capitalization.

“Every Bitcoin we acquire further strengthens Hyperscale Data’s balance sheet and expands our financial flexibility,” Executive Chairman Milton “Todd” Ault III said at the time. “We intend to continue building our Bitcoin position over time.”

A week later, Hyperscale disclosed its first sale, about 150.5 BTC, leaving it with roughly 958.5 BTC as of Aug. 2, even as its Ault Capital Group subsidiary bought about 15 BTC on the open market. The holdings were split between that unit and Sentinum. Ault said then that the company planned to keep a long-term position while tapping it for short-term flexibility, adding, “We are confident in our ability to use Bitcoin as pristine collateral.”

Tuesday’s release closes that chapter. Hyperscale said it still mines bitcoin at its Montana site and expects any bitcoin it holds on its balance sheet going forward to come “primarily” from its own mining operations.

Hyperscale is the latest public company to give up a bitcoin treasury in favor of data center spending. Keel sold its entire bitcoin treasury for $192 million in the third quarter, and Riot and Hut 8 sold thousands of bitcoin to fund AI buildouts and buybacks.

Larger miners are moving the same way. MARA said Monday it sold 13,597 BTC for about $1.1 billion after Sept. 30 and plans to keep selling to fund AI infrastructure, convertible note puts and share repurchases.

Hyperscale’s structure is more complicated than a pure-play miner’s. Its Sentinum subsidiary runs the data center business, while Ault Capital Group, a hybrid private equity firm and operating company, holds a mix of lending, defense, industrial and hotel businesses. Hyperscale expects to divest Ault Capital Group in 2027 and become a data center owner and operator.

The company said it expects to file its third-quarter 10-Q on or before Nov. 23. Because of a newly proposed exchange offer for its Class A common stock, it is also moving its virtual Investor Day from Nov. 12 to Nov. 23, when management plans to update investors on debt reduction and the December energization target.

Hyperscale did not say how much debt the bitcoin proceeds have retired so far or how much remains outstanding.

That put about two-thirds of the new common-stock money into buying back preferred shares. Less than a third went into Bitcoin. STRC’s market-price recovery had yet to become reported STRC ATM financing within that week.

Strategy’s November 2–8 MSTR proceeds went mostly to repurchasing preferred shares. No STRC ATM sales were reported for the week.

STRC buybacks received 2.3 times the common-stock proceeds allocated to Bitcoin.

STRC recorded an historical intraday high price of $100 on November 4 and an unadjusted close of $99.98. Its highs on November 5 and 6 were $99.97 and $99.84, respectively.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Strategy’s July 27 policy announcement said the company would not issue STRC below $100. It also described a long-term objective of healthy, sustained trading near that level. The issuance and dividend policies were statements of intent that could change.

Strategy receives new equity capital when it sells shares and collects proceeds. In a secondary-market trade, a buyer instead pays another investor for an existing share. The market price and the issuer’s financing proceeds therefore describe separate transactions.

For November 2–8, the reported financing came from selling 2,051,348 MSTR shares. That share-sales figure includes shares sold but not yet settled as of November 6.

CryptoSlate’s November 5 coverage described renewed STRC financing as a possibility and included an unconfirmed tracker estimate that issuance might have financed 191 BTC. The subsequent weekly disclosure reports no STRC ATM sales for the period containing November 4.

Strategy bought 1,036 BTC for $81.9 million during the week, at an average $79,012 per coin including fees and expenses. Its Bitcoin purchase disclosure expressly identifies MSTR sale proceeds as the funding source.

Bitcoin purchases absorbed 28.9% of the common-stock proceeds. STRC repurchases took 66.3%, or about 2.3 times the Bitcoin allocation, while USD Cash received 4.8%. That makes the buyback program a substantial competing use of newly raised capital.

It also changes the mix of claims on the company. Issuing MSTR expands the common-share base, while repurchasing STRC reduces preferred claims. Strategy bought back 1,887,464 STRC shares during November 2–8.

The company’s July policy described discounted repurchases as a way to reduce future preferred dividend requirements. That gives the spending an economic purpose alongside its immediate cash cost. The eventual dividend savings depend on future rates and declarations.

The policy also anticipated a greater repurchase pace at deeper discounts, tapering as STRC approached $100. It left decisions subject to liquidity, market conditions and capital-allocation priorities.

The preceding week also paired MSTR fundraising with STRC repurchases, but disclosed a different funding route. In its November 2 filing, Strategy reported $164 million in MSTR proceeds: $65.5 million funded Bitcoin purchases and $98.5 million went to USD Cash. It separately used $208.2 million of USD Cash for STRC repurchases.

For November 2–8, the company explicitly assigned MSTR proceeds directly to the buybacks. The comparison shows a change in the disclosed funding allocation, with common-stock sales financing Bitcoin purchases in both weeks.

At November 8, USD Cash stood at $146.2 million, alongside a separate $4.77 billion reserve for dividends and interest. Strategy used $21.1 million of that reserve to pay preferred dividends during the week.

The next financing development depends on reported issuer sales and the use of their proceeds. STRC’s return to $100 provided a market-price milestone; the November 2–8 allocation shows that MSTR issuance supplied the disclosed money for both Bitcoin accumulation and preferred-share repurchases.

#5131. MARA Sells 13,597 Bitcoin for $1.1 Billion, Says More Sales Are Coming

MARA sold about 40% of its bitcoin stack after Sept. 30, used $750 million to repay all its BTC-backed term loans and said it will keep selling to fund AI infrastructure, convertible note puts and buybacks.

MARA Holdings sold 13,597 bitcoin for about $1.1 billion after the third quarter ended and used $750 million of the cash to pay off all of its bitcoin-backed term loans, the miner said Monday in its Q3 shareholder letter.

The coins went for roughly $81,039 each. MARA held 34,012 BTC on Sept. 30, so the sale cut about 40% of the stack in a matter of weeks.

Repaying the loans returned 17,477 bitcoin that had been pledged as collateral, leaving less than 5% of MARA’s holdings loaned or pledged, the company said. Subtracting the sale from the quarter-end figure implies about 20,400 BTC left, before counting coins mined since.

The letter makes clear the selling is not over. “We intend to continue using bitcoin to build cash for purposes such as AI infrastructure opportunities, to satisfy the puts on our convertible notes that become exercisable next year, and potential share repurchases,” MARA wrote.

The board also authorized a buyback of up to $500 million of common stock after quarter-end.

MARA spent years building one of the largest bitcoin stacks among public companies. The shift away from holding came in stages. The company said it began permitting sales of mined bitcoin in 2025 and in 2026 expanded the policy to cover bitcoin already on its balance sheet.

It sold about 26,984 bitcoin for $1.9 billion in the first nine months of the year, including 3,891 BTC in the third quarter at an average of $75,300. It mined 2,247 BTC in the quarter and bought none.

Year to date, MARA has cut total debt by $1.4 billion, or 37%. It ended September with $946.5 million in cash and about $3.8 billion in cash and bitcoin combined, plus 1,408 BTC lent out that earned about $2.5 million in interest during the quarter.

MARA’s post-quarter sale fetched about $6,800 more per coin than bitcoin’s price near $74,200 on Tuesday morning.

The quarter’s headline profit came from the coins, not the mines. Revenue fell 35% to $164.2 million from $252.4 million a year earlier, even as energized hashrate rose 18% to 71.2 EH/s.

Net income rose to $632.1 million, or $1.28 a diluted share, from $123.1 million, helped by an $885.3 million gain on the fair value of digital assets.

Mining itself got slightly more expensive. Purchased energy cost per bitcoin at MARA’s owned sites was $39,674, up from $39,235 a year earlier, while blocks won rose 2% to 644.

“Bitcoin built the foundation. Power created the opportunity,” the letter opens, describing a strategy to “maximize the value of every megawatt” MARA controls.

MARA said it is targeting two data center leases before year-end but warned that “market conditions are fluid and that timing could slip into next year.” It received FERC approval for its Long Ridge natural gas plant purchase, currently authorized to sell 485 MW, and expects to close in the coming weeks. It also secured rights to develop a Matagorda County, Texas site with up to 2 GW of power capacity, and its Exaion unit signed a four-year, $79 million GPU-as-a-service contract with its first U.S. customer.

The move puts MARA in step with peers pulling away from bitcoin. Riot and Hut 8 sold thousands of bitcoin to fund AI projects and buybacks, Keel sold its entire treasury, and Core Scientific plans to exit mining by year-end.

MARA still describes bitcoin mining as its operating foundation, one that keeps sites earning revenue while higher-value infrastructure is built out. With roughly 13,600 fewer coins on the books and a stated plan to keep selling, the miner that once carried one of the largest corporate stacks is now using that stack to pay for its next business.

#5130. Maternal AI chatbot shows zero postpartum mental health gain

At 12 months, Rosie users showed no significant anxiety or depression advantage over a monthly children's book program.

Access to Rosie, an AI chatbot for maternal health, showed no statistically significant anxiety or depression benefit at 12 months compared with a monthly children's book program in a 400-person randomized trial.

The study, published Nov. 4 in JAMA Network Open, tested access to health advice among pregnant and postpartum participants with generally low starting symptom scores.

Rosie was designed to answer maternal and infant health questions, offer personalized guidance, and provide reminders and educational support. A clinical social worker reviewed distress alerts, contacted participants, and provided resources, so the intervention included human support alongside the chatbot.

Researchers assigned 200 participants to each group. The US study enrolled English-speaking people aged 14 or older who were pregnant or parenting an infant younger than six months and identified as members of racial or ethnic minoritized groups.

Enrollment ran from October 2023 through December 2024, with follow-up completed in December 2025.

The primary measures were self-reported anxiety symptoms on the GAD-7 questionnaire and depression symptoms on the PHQ-9 questionnaire. The 12-month analyses included 361 participants for anxiety and 362 for depression.

The adjusted difference between the chatbot and control groups was 0.10 points for anxiety, with a 95% confidence interval of -0.77 to 0.96. For depression, it was -0.23 points, with an interval of -1.05 to 0.59.

Both intervals include zero, leaving uncertainty about the size and direction of any underlying effect. The trial established neither a clear benefit nor equivalence between the approaches.

Two equally sized randomized groups were compared on anxiety and depression after 12 months. Neither primary outcome showed a statistically significant difference.

Anxiety: adjusted mean difference 0.10 points. The 95% confidence interval crosses zero.

Depression: adjusted mean difference -0.23 points. The 95% confidence interval crosses zero.

Outcomes were self-reported. Rosie included incentives and human support; both groups could contact staff.

Reported adjusted chatbot-minus-control differences and 95% confidence intervals. Interval geometry uses a shared -1.2 to +1.2 questionnaire-point domain, scaled linearly; no effect estimates are recalculated. Each option represents a different symptom questionnaire.

US, English-speaking pregnant/postpartum participants from racial or ethnic minoritized groups, with generally low baseline symptoms. 400 randomized; endpoint samples: anxiety 361, depression 362. Outcomes were self-reported. Rosie included incentives and human support; both groups could contact staff.

Participants received small payments for questions and book rewards for the most active users. The control group received one children's board book per month for a year, and both groups could communicate with study staff.

Those conditions limit how directly the findings translate to routine chatbot use.

Participants generally began with low symptom scores. Among those with available baseline responses, the median anxiety score was 2, and the median depression score was 3. About 11.1% and 9.5% of the participants, respectively, met the study's moderate-to-severe threshold.

Self-reported health outcomes are another limitation, and the authors urged caution about secondary and exploratory findings.

Rosie, delivered with incentives and human backup to this population, showed no statistically significant 12-month advantage on the two primary mental-health measures. Whether other AI interventions or different clinical populations would yield different results remains open.

#5128. Tom Lee’s Bitmine Adds $20M in Ethereum, Seizing on Vitalik’s 'ETH Is Money' Declaration

Tom Lee’s Bitmine Adds $20M in Ethereum, Seizing on Vitalik’s 'ETH Is Money' Declaration

Bitmine Immersion Technologies extended its weekly Ethereum buying streak, adding 9,587 ETH worth roughly $20 million, as chairman Tom Lee pointed to a recent declaration by Ethereum co-founder Vitalik Buterin as validation of the firm’s accumulation strategy.

The NYSE-listed company said Monday its holdings reached 6,070,092 ETH, worth roughly $13.6 billion at a reference price of $2,234 per coin.

That’s 4.97% of Ethereum’s 122.2 million token supply, leaving Bitmine, by its own count, 99% of the way to its goal of owning 5% of the network, a target it calls the “Alchemy of 5%,” reached in just 15 months.

The firm has bought Ethereum every week since launching its treasury strategy on June 30, 2025.

Lee leaned heavily on comments Buterin made at the Devcon conference in Mumbai, where the Ethereum founder said, “I’ve finally made up my mind, ETH is a store of value, ETH is money.” Lee called the declaration “a seismic shift,” arguing it clarifies how ETH will capture value in what he described as a coming “supercycle of tokenization and AI.” (Disclosure: Tom Lee is one of several investors in Dastan, Decrypt’s parent company.)

He contended ETH functions as “pristine and trusted collateral” rather than a cash-flow token, reflecting its position as the layer-1 network favored by financial institutions moving assets onchain.

Buterin elaborated in an X post, laying out why he now sees ETH primarily as a store of value, including his view that fee-burning is weaker than it once seemed and that optimizing for it can conflict with decentralization. Lee framed that reasoning as consistent with Ethereum’s roadmap and its role in the tokenization wave.

Counting cash, other tokens and “moonshot” investments, Bitmine’s total holdings stood at $14.3 billion as of Nov. 8, including 216 Bitcoin, $475 million in cash and marketable securities, a $180 million stake in Beast Industries and a $75 million position in Eightco Holdings, which it bills as a rare public proxy for indirect OpenAI exposure.

Bitmine remains the world’s largest Ethereum treasury and the second-largest crypto treasury overall, trailing only Michael Saylor’s Strategy, which the release said holds about 849,327 BTC. Bitmine has 5,227,037 ETH, roughly 86% of its holdings, staked through its MAVAN platform, with projected annualized staking revenue of about $359 million.

The firm also recently argued the muted market reaction to the record Binance hack showed crypto’s growing resilience.

#5127. Mercado Bitcoin begins post-hack reopening without a timeline for withdrawals

Customers can view their pre-hack balances as access returns in phases. The exchange has yet to say when withdrawals will resume.

Crypto exchange Mercado Bitcoin began restoring read-only access to customer accounts in phases on Monday, a week after attackers drained an estimated $49 million from the platform. The exchange has yet to say when customers will be able to withdraw funds.

A Mercado Bitcoin spokesperson told Cointelegraph that access would begin at 19:00 UTC, with all accounts expected to be viewable by 21:00 UTC. Customers will be able to see their holdings as they stood on Nov. 2, the day of the hack.

In a video posted to the exchange’s X account, Fabricio Tota, vice president of new business, said Mercado Bitcoin hoped read-only access would reassure customers that their assets were still recorded on the platform.

Mercado Bitcoin, one of Brazil’s largest crypto exchanges, told Brazilian magazine Veja in late October that it had more than 4.8 million customers and had processed over 215 billion Brazilian reais ($39 billion) in cumulative transactions. The exchange says it is using its own funds to replace the stolen assets, but has yet to give a date for withdrawals to resume.

Tota explained that the company opted for “a more conservative approach” to reactivating accounts “to ensure our return happens with the utmost security.” The caution follows concerns from blockchain security analysts that the attackers were able to access addresses, draining funds through standard transfers rather than a smart contract exploit.

Blockchain analysts traced some of the stolen funds to customer deposit addresses, but Mercado Bitcoin says customers won’t bear the losses. “The vast majority of assets, in fact, nearly all of them, were never even touched by anyone; they were not even involved in the incident,” Tota said in the video.

On Nov. 4, Mercado Bitcoin pledged to cover all losses with company funds, bolstered by a $20 million investment from stablecoin issuer Tether in July. Tota highlighted that a portion of the stolen funds were also frozen in the hours after the company announced an attack had taken place.

The next phase of recovery will require users to confirm their registration details on the platform before they can move funds. The exchange plans to restore withdrawals via Pix, Brazil’s instant payment system, initially allowing customers to withdraw only to accounts in their own name. Tota gave no date for either step.

Mercado Bitcoin has yet to explain how the attackers gained access to its wallets. Blockchain data company Bitquery found that as of Nov. 4, wallets it linked to the attacker held most of the stolen funds that had escaped freezes, including 106.07 BTC.

#5124. Trump Media (DJT) Pledges 73% of Its Bitcoin to an Options Program as $1 Billion Note Deadline Nears

Trump Media's Q3 10-Q shows 11,385 of its roughly 15,649 bitcoin pledged to an options program whose counterparty can reuse the coins, with nearly all the rest backing $1 billion of notes holders can put back Nov. 30.

Trump Media & Technology Group has pledged about 73% of its bitcoin to an options program in which the counterparty can reuse the coins, the company disclosed in its third-quarter 10-Q filed Monday, three weeks before holders of its $1 billion in convertible notes can demand cash.

The Truth Social parent held approximately 15,649 BTC on Sept. 30, Chief Financial Officer Phillip Juhan said on Monday’s earnings call, along with 7 million shares of BlackRock’s IBIT. Of those coins, 11,385.20 were pledged to what the company calls its bitcoin yield management program.

The filing says that program requires Trump Media to post bitcoin as collateral “that the counterparty can rehypothecate at their sole discretion.” The pledged coins carried a fair value of $951.4 million at quarter-end. The 10-Q does not name the counterparty.

Another 4,260.73 BTC, worth $356.1 million on Sept. 30, sits as collateral for the convertible notes and cannot be withdrawn or distributed until the indenture’s conditions are met.

Together, the two buckets account for about 15,646 BTC, nearly all of the stack Juhan described.

Trump Media sells and buys covered calls and puts on bitcoin, settled in bitcoin, to “generate yield on these assets and increase our principal balance of bitcoin,” the filing says. Premiums arrive in bitcoin and are “immediately rehypothecated to our digital asset manager to reinvest.”

At quarter-end it had written calls referencing 4,838.40 BTC at strikes from $68,000 to $92,000 and puts on 1,500 BTC at $75,000 to $80,000, all expiring in October.

A risk factor spells out the trade-off. The company says its yield counterparties “may not be rated” by credit agencies, that it is limited in its ability to sell or use deployed bitcoin while the arrangements are open, and that where they are unsecured “we may be unable to recover deployed bitcoin in a counterparty insolvency.”

Juhan said Trump Media bought 4,359 bitcoin during the quarter with proceeds from a partial sale of its IBIT position, part of a shift toward “third-party institutional” management of its treasury.

The bitcoin rally in the third quarter turned Trump Media profitable on paper. Net income was $409.7 million, driven by a $378.1 million gain on digital assets as bitcoin climbed to about $84,000 on Sept. 30 from about $59,000 on June 30. Adjusted EBITDA was still a loss of $12.3 million, and the company lost $234.2 million over the first nine months.

Prices have since moved against the stack. With bitcoin near $74,200 Tuesday morning, the 15,649 coins are worth about $1.16 billion, roughly $145 million less than at quarter-end.

The bigger test is the notes. Holders of the $1 billion in 0% senior secured convertibles, due May 2028, can require Trump Media to buy them back at full principal for cash on Nov. 30. General Counsel Scott Glabe said notices have gone out and holders have until near the end of the month to respond, so the company cannot yet say how much it will owe.

Interim CEO Kevin McGurn said the plan is “a mix of cash and credit” rather than new equity. “We want to preserve as much of our Bitcoin as possible to continue our yielding strategy and allow that asset to recover,” he said.

The 10-Q says the company may enter a new credit facility to refinance some or all of the notes, but adds that “there can be no assurance” it can do so on acceptable terms. Juhan put gross financial assets at $2.3 billion, including $182 million in cash, $211 million in short-term investments and $1.7 billion in bitcoin and bitcoin-related assets.

Trump Media is not the only treasury company under pressure. Strategy, the largest corporate holder, slipped below its average cost on Monday. Trump Media also faces more political scrutiny after Democrats won Congress, with party leaders vowing to target Trump family crypto ventures and incoming Senate Banking Chair Elizabeth Warren preparing her own oversight push.

Shareholders vote Dec. 14 on the company’s planned merger with fusion energy firm TAE Technologies, which management expects to close by year-end.

#5122. Miami-Dade County Now Accepts Bitcoin for Property Taxes

Miami-Dade County Tax Collector Dariel Fernandez said Monday his office has begun accepting bitcoin for property tax payments, both online and in person, while converting every transaction into dollars before the money reaches the county.

Fernandez announced the change in a statement on the Tax Collector’s website, calling it a way to give property owners another option without putting public funds into the asset. Payers can select a cryptocurrency option during online checkout or request it at the counter, then connect a supported exchange account or digital wallet and authorize the payment.

A third-party system converts the bitcoin into U.S. dollars at the time of payment, the office said, so it never holds, invests in or manages any cryptocurrency. The office said it still receives the full dollar amount owed and records the transactions in its standard payment reports, without county workers maintaining digital wallets.

“Government must keep up with the times and the needs of the people we serve,” Fernandez said. “By accepting Bitcoin as a payment option, we are giving property owners another way to pay their taxes, whether online or in person, while continuing to offer the traditional payment methods they already know and trust.”

He added that the office is “embracing new technology without compromising our responsibility to protect taxpayer dollars.” Whether someone pays with bitcoin or a conventional method, he said, “our office receives the full amount owed in U.S. dollars.”

The office did not name the payment processor or say whether bitcoin payments carry a convenience fee. Card and mobile-wallet payments already carry a nonrefundable 2.50% fee online, with a $2.50 minimum, the office’s payment-methods page shows. Traditional options, including checks, cards, cash and fee-free eChecks, remain available. The announcement covers property taxes, and the option is voluntary.

JUST IN: 🇺🇸 Miami-Dade now accepts Bitcoin for property taxes and can be paid both online and in person 👀 pic.twitter.com/lI285z4B79

The change landed in the middle of Florida’s property tax collection season. Miami-Dade opened the 2026 tax roll on Oct. 30 and is urging owners to pay by Nov. 30 to lock in a 4% early-payment discount under Florida law. The discount falls to 3% in December, 2% in January and 1% in February, with the full amount due in March. Unpaid bills generally become delinquent April 1.

Under the office’s existing rules, discounts are based on the date a payment is submitted online or in person. Fernandez has been promoting a “Save the Max” campaign around that calendar, noting that an eligible $5,000 tax bill would save $200 if paid in November. Payments can be made at the office’s 13 locations, through T-Connect mobile units or online around the clock at mdctaxcollector.gov.

The move closes a loop the county opened years ago. In 2021, Miami-Dade Commissioner Danielle Cohen Higgins put forward a resolution to study letting residents pay county taxes and fees with bitcoin, after Miami Mayor Francis Suarez pushed to let residents pay city fees and taxes in bitcoin and explored paying city workers in it. The city of Miami also launched MiamiCoin, a token tied to the Stacks protocol, as part of that campaign.

Fernandez, elected in November 2024, had earlier served on Miami-Dade’s Cryptocurrency Task Force, his official biography notes.

The office called Miami-Dade one of the first government agencies in the country to accept the payments. Colorado has taken cryptocurrency for state taxes since 2022, converting it to dollars through a third party, and Utah later opened a similar option for individual income taxes. Detroit announced in 2024 that it would start accepting cryptocurrency for city taxes and fees in mid-2025, also settling in dollars.

Florida lawmakers, meanwhile, revived a 2026 bill to create a state Strategic Cryptocurrency Reserve after a 2025 effort was withdrawn. The Senate measure, CS/SB 1038, died in an appropriations committee in March and never became law.

For now, Miami-Dade is offering a narrower door: bitcoin can be used to pay the property tax bill, and the county keeps none of it.

#5120. Vitalik Buterin Moves On From ‘Ultrasound Money,’ Says ETH’s Value Is Stability

Vitalik Buterin Moves On From ‘Ultrasound Money,’ Says ETH’s Value Is Stability

Ethereum co-founder Vitalik Buterin wants people to stop valuing ETH as a fee-burning token and start treating it as a store of value, arguing that the burn was "never" as strong as it seemed. Binji Pande, founding member of Ethlabs, told Decrypt that he agrees, claiming that it is a more "future-proof store of value" than Bitcoin.

It's a notable shift in messaging from the Ethereum community, after ETH was branded as "ultrasound money" when the network started burning a portion of every transaction fee in 2021. The argument was that as more people used Ethereum, fewer coins would exist in circulation, and fewer coins meant a higher price. Now, however, Buterin claims that isn't as important as people may think.

"We should not feel sad if fee burn is < 10% of breakeven indefinitely; the value of ETH comes from elsewhere," Buterin tweeted on Sunday. That value, he argued, is stability, which comes from Ethereum being decentralized, resistant to censorship, and having strong security.

Expanding on why I believe "ETH as store of value (SoV)" makes much more sense to focus on than "ETH as fee-burning token".

1. ETH as fee-burning token never was as strong as it seemed2. Optimizing for ETH as fee-burning token would demand Ethereum and…

— vitalik.eth (@VitalikButerin) November 8, 2026

Those in favour of the fee-burning structure argued that fees gave ETH a price floor, much like a company's stock may be backed by revenue and profits. From 2021 to 2023, Ethereum burned millions of ETH every year, but Buterin said this was "never a true floor." Most of these burns, he said, came from internal crypto uses like lending and silly NFT purchases.

Connor Howe, co-founder and CEO of infrastructure provider Enso, told Decrypt that Ethereum's layer-2 expansion made the fee-burning argument worse. Connor said that a significant portion of activity has moved onto layer-2s, and users pay "almost nothing" to settle transactions. To optimize a fee-burning token, he added, you'd also want to optimise for higher gas prices—which no one wants.

"If we decide we actually want to optimize ETH for burning fees to justify a present-day ~$270B market cap, then Ethereum would essentially be functioning as a world-scale major corporation," Buterin said. In doing that, Ethereum would lose one of its major selling points: decentralization.

"I agree with Vitalik, and I also believe that ETH is best positioned as a future-proof store of value," Ethlabs' Pande told Decrypt. He highlighted that the network is censorship-resistant, that it has "great distribution," is widely available, and that it is "at the center of future growing sectors such as tokenization, Stablecoins, and DeFi."

A store of value is an asset that holds its worth over time, meaning investors can put their money into it without fearing it will disappear. Historically, gold has been seen as the ultimate store of value, and Bitcoin supporters have pitched it as a "digital gold."

Pande, however, argues that both fall short compared to Ethereum.

"Gold has held the SoV role for a long time, but given the world we live in now is much more globalized, we need increased portability, something gold simply cannot offer," Pande told Decrypt. "Storing gold is simply not useful either past a certain point; you reach physical limits."

When it comes to Bitcoin as a store of value, Pande expressed concern that it isn't "future-proof" as AI and quantum threats loom.

Ethereum has been preparing for those threats for a while. In January, the Ethereum Foundation formed a dedicated post-quantum team and, more recently, researchers have debated the potential AI-powered threats. By contrast, the Bitcoin community appears more fractured on how to tackle the quantum threat.

"Security is ingrained more deeply into Ethereum’s culture than anywhere else," Julian Ma, co-founder of Ethlabs, told Decrypt. "AI being used for cyberattacks and potentially breaking cryptography strengthens ETH's position as a programmable store-of-value asset because the years of preparation and large investments into security and formal verification of the Ethereum protocol and its applications are about to pay off."

Pande added that newer assets can’t compete with ETH as a store of value, because there’s no way to verify they’re fairly distributed. The Ethereum Foundation owns less than 0.2% of ETH, he said, and the token has traded for 11 years.

“Trying to distribute or mine a new asset as a SoV in the lord’s 2026 is like trying to hide a sack of meat in a sea of piranhas,” he said. “It can and WILL get centralized.”

Not everyone thinks Ethereum’s leadership got here quickly enough, though. Howe said the Ethereum Foundation “needs to start to actually listen and look at the applications building on top of their network.”

“I think he is right,” Howe said of Buterin's recent tweet, “and I think it took too long to say it.”

#5118. Formation Securities plans Solana stock trading venue under SEC exemption

The proposed venue would offer 24/7 trading of tokenized shares from Galaxy Digital, Exodus and Forward Industries, with a launch planned for early 2027.

Formation Securities, a newly established company licensing technology from decentralized exchange Orca, has notified the US Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) of plans to launch a tokenized stock trading venue on Solana in early 2027.

The company said Monday that tokenized shares of Galaxy Digital, Exodus Movement and Forward Industries are expected to be available at launch. The venue would operate under the SEC’s temporary Innovation Exemption announced in September, which allows verified investors to trade certain US-listed shares around the clock while retaining voting, dividend and other shareholder rights.

Superstate and Securitize are expected to make tokenized shares available on the marketplace, which Formation says it intends to develop in cooperation with public companies. The venue would use automated market maker (AMM) technology, allowing investors to hold securities in their own wallets and settle trades onchain against stablecoins and other eligible assets.

Traditional transfer agents would continue to maintain official shareholder records, preserving companies’ direct relationships with shareholders.

Solana is already among the largest blockchains for tokenized equities, with approximately $575 million in tokenized stock value as of Monday, compared with $747 million on Ethereum and $1.7 billion on BNB Chain, according to RWA.xyz data.

Related: Robinhood’s Tenev warns US tokenization uncertainty deters ‘serious assets’

Formation’s issuer-focused approach comes amid growing debate over how publicly traded shares should be brought onchain. As of early November, more than 50 companies had objected to having their shares tokenized on OKXICE, a proposed venue backed by crypto exchange OKX and New York Stock Exchange parent Intercontinental Exchange.

Under the SEC’s Innovation Exemption, trading venues must notify issuers in writing and wait at least 30 calendar days before trading shares tokenized by unaffiliated third parties, giving companies an opportunity to object. Nasdaq warned in September that tokenized stock trading could have unforeseen consequences for liquidity, price discovery and long-term shareholder value.

#5116. North Korean hackers create memecoins to launder funds from Binance hack: Elliptic

The blockchain firm traced stolen funds through six new tokens on NEAR, where hackers used liquidity pools to obscure transfers between wallets.

North Korean hackers created memecoins to launder cryptocurrency stolen in the $6.3 billion Binance hack, routing roughly $430,000 through six newly launched tokens on the NEAR blockchain, according to a report from blockchain analytics firm Elliptic.

Between Nov. 5 and 7, the hackers created at least six tokens on NEAR and used liquidity pools on decentralized exchanges to move stolen funds between wallets, Elliptic said. The tokens attracted almost no outside trading and were abandoned shortly after launch.

The hackers used one set of wallets to swap stolen NEAR tokens for the new memecoins, then used another set to sell those memecoins back into the pools for NEAR. The swaps let them move funds without direct wallet-to-wallet transfers, though the transactions remained traceable onchain.

How stolen NEAR moved between wallets through liquidity pools. Source: Elliptic

Elliptic also found that five of the six token creators were funded with proceeds from earlier token sales just two to 13 minutes before the next launch, linking the tokens in a chain of transactions.

“North Korea’s launderers rarely rely on a single technique,” Elliptic co-founder Tom Robinson wrote in the report. “Instead, they layer many cheap techniques, each adding a little more delay.”

Elliptic said the same laundering technique was also used following the $1.5 billion Bybit hack in 2025, when North Korean-linked actors moved at least $24 million through a newly created token on the TRON blockchain.

At the time of Elliptic’s analysis, about 11.8 million NEAR stolen in the Binance hack remained unmoved. Elliptic valued those funds at $43.7 million.
