#5062. Nvidia, Jensen Huang Sued by Shareholders Over Alleged Ties to Suspected Chinese Spy

Nvidia, Jensen Huang Sued by Shareholders Over Alleged Ties to Suspected Chinese Spy

Nvidia and CEO Jensen Huang have been sued by a shareholder who accuses them of defrauding investors by concealing Huang's alleged relationship with a suspected Chinese spy.

The proposed class action, filed in the U.S. District Court for the Northern District of California on Monday, accuses Nvidia and Huang of making materially false and misleading statements about the company's dealings with Megaspeed, a Singapore-based Nvidia customer, according to the complaint. It alleges that Huang "had an affair with Alice Huang, a Megaspeed executive and suspected Chinese spy," and that the defendants failed to disclose that this raised national security concerns posing a material risk to Nvidia's reputation and business prospects.

The plaintiff, Mark Kistenmacher, seeks to represent investors who bought Nvidia stock between October 9, 2025, and November 8, 2026. The suit accuses Nvidia and Huang of violating Section 10(b) of the Securities Exchange Act and Rule 10b-5, and Huang of violating Section 20(a), which can hold executives liable alongside a company. It seeks unspecified damages and a jury trial. Kistenmacher is represented by The Rosen Law Firm.

The complaint targets four statements. Two came from Nvidia spokesperson John Rizzo in October 2025, when he told The New York Times that Nvidia's employees had "found no evidence of diversion" at Megaspeed and that Huang's visits to customer events were routine. A third came from Rizzo last month, when he said Huang's interactions with Alice Huang were "not unusual or unexpected." The fourth was Huang's own description of Nvidia's dealings with Megaspeed as "normal and not unusual" in an interview with Bloomberg on October 30. Each was misleading, the suit alleges, because of Huang's relationship with Alice Huang.

Alice Huang, a former Megaspeed executive, is not related to Jensen Huang, Reuters reported. The New York Times reported on Sunday that Nvidia opened its investigation after Huang acknowledged to the company's board that he had been intimate with Alice Huang. The newspaper also reported that U.S. Secretary of State Marco Rubio oversaw the drafting of a classified memo this spring warning top administration officials about the relationship, which was circulated ahead of U.S. President Donald Trump's trip to China in May.

Decrypt has reached out to Nvidia for comment. The allegations in the complaint have not been tested in court, and it is common for shareholders to sue companies after sharp share price declines.

The lawsuit was filed five minutes after U.S. markets closed on Monday, according to Reuters. Nvidia shares closed down 4.3% at $178.67 that day, according to Yahoo Finance, reducing its market value by about $190 billion, after having slid as much as 8% earlier in the session. The stock was flat on the day at $179.70 in premarket trading on Tuesday.

Nvidia's board opened an independent investigation on Sunday into reports that Huang was the target of a foreign intelligence effort, saying it had no reason to believe that national security or company secrets had been compromised. In an email to staff the same day, Huang apologized for being unfaithful to his wife, without naming the person involved.

The New York Times reported last month that U.S. officials had concluded Alice Huang was a Chinese intelligence asset, and the Financial Times later reported that a U.S. government memo said Jensen Huang had been romantically involved with her. China's embassy in Washington has dismissed the allegations as "groundless smears."

Trump weighed in on Monday. "I asked him about that and he said he had absolutely no idea that the person in question was a spy," the president told reporters, according to The Guardian. He added that the FBI was "looking into it in terms of something else," without elaborating. A day earlier, Trump told reporters he did not regret his decision not to act on the intelligence, and that he believed Huang should stay on as CEO. "I think he's an honorable guy," he said, according to the Times.

#5059. The Best Gifts for Wives That She’ll Never Forget

To inspire you, we asked the wives on our team what gifts they wanted this year.

#5058. BitGo Q3 profit nearly doubles on crypto gains as revenue falls 51%

BitGo’s $71 million unrealized gain on its crypto holdings helped nearly double its net income, but revenue and earnings came in below preliminary analyst expectations.

US-listed digital asset infrastructure company BitGo Holdings reported $44.1 million in third-quarter net income, nearly double a year earlier, as a rise in the value of its crypto holdings offset a $19.2 million operating loss.

The crypto custodian recorded a $71.5 million unrealized gain on digital assets in the quarter ended Sept. 30, according to its quarterly filing. A year earlier, it reported operating income of $3.9 million.

Revenue fell 51% from a year earlier to $2.85 billion as digital asset sales slowed, BitGo said Monday. The company generally records the full proceeds of those sales as revenue and accounts for the costs separately. Revenue after direct costs fell 32% to $37.3 million.

BitGo missed Wall Street estimates for both revenue and earnings in the third quarter, with revenue coming in 32% below the $4.2 billion consensus shared by US financial research firm Zacks Investment Research.

Its shares rose 4.9% on Monday to close at $5.10, reversing some of the 60% decline seen since early in the year, according to Yahoo Finance.

The company reported holding $143 million of cash and cash equivalents and 2,399 Bitcoin (BTC) in corporate treasury holdings, with a fair value of $200 million as of Sept. 30.

BitGo stock price, year-to-date chart. Source: Yahoo Finance

In June, BitGo laid off about 15% of its staff to refocus on areas including trading, stablecoins and artificial intelligence.

#5057. NEAR AI says SGLang vulnerability exposed user account details in security breach

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

NEAR AI explained that it found no indication that stored Private Chat conversations or uploaded files were accessed.

NEAR AI has contained a security breach after an attacker exploited a remote code execution vulnerability in the open-source SGLang inference engine running on its servers. NEAR AI is the AI product and infrastructure arm of the NEAR NEAR Protocol (NEAR) ecosystem.

According to NEAR AI's security incident report published on Monday, the attacker gained code execution on one inference server from the vulnerability and reached an internal system behind a previously deprecated Private Chat feature.

From there, the attacker downloaded account details — including email addresses, names, and in some cases plan and balance information — for approximately 100 users.

The attacker also accessed 18 agents on a legacy NEAR AI Agents deployment that was not running in the company’s confidential computing environment, including their chat history, settings, and logs. Two Cloud customers had their API keys exposed in logs, while those keys have since been revoked.

Its report says that on Nov. 8, the attacker could have observed user requests being processed on the compromised server, including those in confidential mode. However, NEAR AI found no indication that stored Private Chat conversations or uploaded files were accessed.

"Confidential computing protects data from the operator and the host. It does not protect against a vulnerability in the software running inside the environment, and that is what happened here," NEAR AI said. "The SGLang vulnerability let the party run code inside one inference server and access requests in processing on that server, including confidential ones."

The company said it has closed the access, replaced the affected credentials, and patched the vulnerability across all inference servers. Access to NEAR AI Cloud, which was paused during the investigation, has been restored.

NEAR AI also revoked active sessions and administrative credentials, rotated agent tokens, took Private Chat offline, and completed a full forensic review along with remaining credential rotations.

"We are now treating the inference software and the services around the enclave with the same rigor as the enclave itself when running security analysis, and the code, dependencies, and deployment configurations for our confidential inference stack remain public so that anyone can verify what is running," NEAR AI said.

The company has reached out to the affected users and the owners of the 18 agents individually.

#5056. Binance Halts MEXC Transfers as FinCEN Cuts Exchange Off From US Financial System

Binance Halts MEXC Transfers as FinCEN Cuts Exchange Off From US Financial System

Binance has stopped processing transactions involving MEXC, one of the world's largest crypto exchanges, days after the U.S. Treasury Department designated MEXC a primary money laundering concern.

"Effective immediately, users should not send funds to, receive funds from, or otherwise transact through Binance involving MEXC, MEXC Global, MEXC affiliates or related entities," Binance tweeted on Tuesday.

Binance is applying enhanced compliance measures following FinCEN’s designation of MEXC as a Primary Money Laundering Concern.

Effective immediately, users should not send funds to, receive funds from, or otherwise transact through Binance involving MEXC, MEXC Global, MEXC…

Any request to send funds from a Binance account to MEXC will be declined, according to a Binance announcement that lists 13 entities covered by the restriction, including MEXC Global Limited, MEXC Estonia OÜ and MEXC Singapore Pte Ltd. Deposits received from those entities will be temporarily withheld for compliance review, and users could face further restrictions, including being offboarded from Binance, the exchange said.

MEXC ranks ninth among spot exchanges on CoinMarketCap, which scores platforms on traffic, liquidity and trading volume, with about $812.5 million in spot volume over the past 24 hours. Binance ranks first, with about $6.4 billion.

The Treasury's Financial Crimes Enforcement Network (FinCEN) named MEXC a primary money laundering concern last Friday, in connection with Russian illicit finance. The order, issued under the Combating Russian Money Laundering Act, bars U.S. financial institutions from processing fund transfers involving the exchange.

FinCEN alleges that MEXC processed at least $16 billion in suspicious transactions between May 2021 and May 2026, including nearly $1.5 billion in direct and indirect exposure to Russian sanctions evasion, and described its anti-money laundering controls as severely deficient.

The order takes effect 30 days after its publication in the Federal Register.

Binance is not the only exchange to respond. OKX has also warned its users against transferring funds to MEXC, Finance Magnates reported.

#5046. Binance Warns of Apple iOS Flaw That Could Expose iPhone Users' Private Keys

Binance Warns of Apple iOS Flaw That Could Expose iPhone Users' Private Keys

Binance has warned iPhone users about a vulnerability in Apple's iOS that it says could let malicious apps read files stored by other apps, putting crypto wallet seed phrases, private keys and login credentials at risk.

The flaw, dubbed "worse_query," affects iOS 26.7.1, 27.0.1 and 27.2, the exchange tweeted on Tuesday. "This is an iOS-level issue, not unique to Binance," it said.

A vulnerability (worse_query) affecting iOS 26.7.1, 27.0.1, and 27.2 allows malicious apps to read local files from other apps, putting seed phrases, private keys, and login credentials at risk across the entire ecosystem. This is an iOS-level issue, not unique to Binance.…

Binance urged users to delete any apps installed from untrusted sources, such as sideloaded or manually signed app files, and to update iOS and all apps to the latest official versions. Anyone who suspects a breach should move their funds to a new wallet created on a clean device and reset all active login sessions, it said.

iPhone apps normally run inside a sandbox, which stops them from accessing other apps' private files. A researcher who goes by forcequitOS published worse_query on GitHub as a sandbox escape for iOS 26.0 through 26.7.2 and iOS 27.2, describing it as a proof of concept for developers that "isn't useful by itself." Among the locations it can reach is the folder where iOS apps store their data, according to the repository, which calls it the "direct successor" to an earlier exploit, bad_query. There have been no confirmed reports of crypto being stolen using the flaw.

Apple released iOS 27.2.1 on Monday, a day after the proof of concept was published. Its security notes list fixes for nine vulnerabilities, including an authorization issue in App Intents through which "an app may be able to access sensitive user data," as well as several Bluetooth and iMessage flaws.

However, the notes do not mention worse_query or its researcher, and Apple has not publicly confirmed whether the update addresses the flaw Binance described. At least one security expert believes it does. 23pds, chief information security officer at blockchain security firm SlowMist, urged users to update to iOS 27.2.1, saying in a tweet that the update would block the worse_query sandbox escape, along with other threats.

It is not the first time attackers have targeted Apple devices. Earlier this year, Google warned of iOS malware that targeted crypto apps on unpatched devices, while in August, the Dutch national cyber agency said hackers were exploiting a flaw in Apple's macOS Screen Sharing feature to plant Monero miners on Macs.

#5045. Bitcoin price eyes $74K as Fed rate-hike bets hit 96% after Waller speech

Bitcoin price action and stock futures trended lower after Fed governor Christopher Waller said that he would vote to raise interest rates by 0.25% at the December FOMC meeting.

Bitcoin (BTC) fell toward $74,000, and US stock futures dropped on Tuesday as a senior Federal Reserve official voiced support for hiking interest rates.

Data from TradingView showed BTC/USD reaching $74,080 on Bitstamp, down 1.3% on the day.

BTC/USD one-hour chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

US stock market futures leaned lower as Fed governor Christopher Waller said that he would support hiking interest rates by 25 basis points at the next meeting of the Federal Open Market Committee (FOMC) on Dec. 9. At the time of writing, Nasdaq 100 futures were down 0.4%.

His decision, he added, would not be swayed by lower-than-expected inflation data. The October prints of the US Consumer Price Index (CPI) is due for release at 8.30 am Eastern time on Tuesday. It is expected to show a month-on-month increase of 0.3% versus September when the increase was 0.6%, pushed higher by rising fuel costs.

“While I am hoping for an improvement over September, even if this occurs, I would still support a 25 basis point increase in the FOMC’s policy rate at our December meeting,” Waller commented in a speech at the Bank of England Watchers Conference in London.

While CPI is particularly sensitive to fuel-price volatility, Waller also flagged high readings from the Personal Consumption Expenditures (PCE) index, known to be the Fed’s “preferred” inflation gauge and which he confirmed was the “measure targeted by the FOMC.”

“Let’s just say it. September’s inflation data were bad. There was no silver lining,” he said, referring to PCE rising 0.5% month-on-month and 3.7% year-on-year, respectively.

Following the speech, markets immediately repriced odds of a 25-basis-point hike in December from 76.3% to 96.3%, per data from CME Group’s FedWatch Tool.

Fed target-rate probability comparison for December FOMC meeting (screenshot). Source: CME Group

Financial policy tightening measures traditionally act as headwinds for crypto and risk asset prices as they imply a reduction in liquidity and associated capital inflows.

Related: Bitcoin, tech stocks brush off Nvidia nerves as analyst mulls $68K target

Commenting on the impetus for the expected slowdown in CPI upside for October, Christopher Hodge, chief US economist at Natixis CIB Americas, pointed to price declines in both fuel and shelter.

“We think the slowdown in core CPI will stem from a reversion of shelter prices, primarily owners’ equivalent rents and lodging away from home. Similarly, the stabilization in diesel prices should help to quell price pressures in energy-intensive goods,” he wrote, quoted by CNBC.

The Bureau of Labor Statistics (BLS), which publishes CPI, confirmed that Tuesday’s release would include only month-on-month statistics due to the 2025 US government shutdown reducing data availability. CPI data for October 2025 was not issued for the same reason.

#5042. HIVE reports $79 million quarterly net loss as bitcoin mining revenue falls and AI business grows

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

HIVE booked a $31.2 million impairment charge against its bitcoin mining equipment during the quarter and reported $185 million in contracted GPU cloud annualized run-rate revenue.

Bitcoin mining firm HIVE Digital Technologies HIVE Digital Technologies Ltd. (HIVE) reported a $78.7 million net loss in its fiscal second quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with a $15.8 million loss a year earlier.

The company generated $84.9 million in revenue, down 2.7% year over year, according to its latest earnings release on Monday.

HIVE received 1,070 BTC in mining rewards during the quarter, a 49% increase from 718 BTC in the same period last year. Mining revenue was $76 million, down from $82.1 million a year ago. Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) averaged $71,003 during the quarter, against $114,382 in the year-earlier period, which HIVE cited as the main reason for the decline in its 10-Q filing with the Securities and Exchange Commission.

HIVE also booked a $31.2 million impairment after assessing the recoverable value of its mining equipment. Depreciation amounted to another $54.9 million, and adjusted EBITDA was $24.8 million.

Meanwhile, the company's BUZZ High Performance Computing business generated $8.8 million in revenue, up 70.7% from $5.2 million last year. HIVE deployed an Nvidia B200 GPU cluster at its Manitoba site in Canada during the period, with GPU marketplace demand contributing to the increase.

In June, The Block reported that the company had made $297.8 million in revenue in fiscal 2026. High-performance computing accounted for a record $19.5 million of that amount.

The company said it has approximately $40 million in live GPU cloud annualized run-rate revenue. Its contracted figure is now approximately $185 million, including capacity that has yet to enter service. HIVE previously signed a three-year agreement with Bell AI Fabric supporting Cohere in June for 2,304 Nvidia Grace Blackwell GPUs. In August, HIVE also signed a $350 million deal to supply 2,016 NVIDIA Blackwell Ultra GPUs to an unnamed enterprise customer over five years.

CEO Aydin Kilic said Monday that the company had secured $129 million in financing for the Bell and Cohere GPU purchase, and he expects those GPUs to ship before the end of 2026. HIVE's near-term goal is $200 million in GPU cloud ARR in the fourth quarter of 2026, followed by a longer-term target of $700 million in total HPC ARR by the end of 2028.

The company ended September with $139 million in cash and cash equivalents and $22.6 million in digital currencies, Kilic said. HIVE currently operates approximately 440 MW of power capacity and expects to energize another 100 MW at Yguazú in Paraguay in the fourth quarter.

#5041. Ex-employee sues Ethereum Foundation and Vitalik Buterin over firing

Kayleen Fulton alleges she was fired less than two weeks after disclosing a cancer diagnosis and helping organize a staff letter challenging a requirement to endorse the foundation’s new mandate.

A former Ethereum Foundation employee has sued the organization, along with Ethereum co-founder Vitalik Buterin and four others.

Kayleen Fulton alleges she was fired after disclosing a cancer diagnosis and organizing a staff letter challenging a directive tied to the foundation’s new mandate.

Fulton, who led the foundation’s protocol support team and is known in the Ethereum community as “Nixo,” filed the complaint on Nov. 4 in the United States District Court for the District of Colorado. She alleges that Bastian Aue, a co-executive director at the time, fired her on April 6, 13 days after she disclosed her diagnosis. The complaint also alleges Buterin helped adopt and enforce the directive as a board member.

Fulton said she and colleague Trent Van Epps organized a letter in response to the mandate with 53 signers. It backed the mandate’s goals while objecting to a requirement that employees affirm it to keep their jobs. The complaint alleges that days later, on April 6, Aue fired Fulton and Van Epps 30 minutes apart. Fulton alleges disability discrimination, retaliation and interference with her medical leave rights and is seeking lost pay and damages.

The complaint also says some employees objected to a reference to ritual suicide in the mandate and quotes Aue as telling staff that the language was figurative.

In a May 14 letter quoted in the complaint, a lawyer for the foundation’s Singapore affiliate, Ethereum Asia Pacific Limited, denied that Fulton had been subjected to unlawful conduct or retaliation and said her separation agreement was binding. Cointelegraph reached out to the foundation, Fulton and Van Epps for comment, but had not received a response by publication.

#5039. Morning Minute: Vitalik Recasts ETH as a Store of Value

Morning Minute: Vitalik Recasts ETH as a Store of Value

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

🏛️ Vitalik Recasts ETH as a Store of Value

Vitalik Buterin spent Sunday on X telling Ethereum holders to stop valuing ETH as a fee-burning token and start valuing it as a store of value. “We should not feel sad if fee burn is less than 10% of breakeven indefinitely,” he wrote. “The value of ETH comes from elsewhere.”

That elsewhere is stability, which he ties to decentralization, censorship resistance and security. It replaces the ultrasound money pitch, the idea that network usage burns supply faster than staking creates it. The burn has not kept up in years. Through the first nine months of 2026 it offset about 2% of issuance while supply grew 0.64%, mostly because Ethereum’s own rollup roadmap moved activity and fees off the mainnet.

Buterin’s argument is that this is fine, and that chasing the burn would be worse. Maximizing fee revenue means wanting congestion and expensive blockspace. He warned it would turn Ethereum into “a world-scale major corporation” at the cost of what makes it worth using. The burn, he added, was “never a true floor” and came mostly from internal crypto churn like lending and “silly NFT purchases.”

Supporters are already building out the new frame. Ethlabs co-founder Binji Pande calls ETH a future-proof store of value and argues it beats Bitcoin on quantum and AI risk, citing eleven years of trading history and an Ethereum Foundation that holds under 0.2% of supply. Julian Ma of Ethlabs makes the related case that Ethereum’s security culture positions it as a programmable store of value as AI pressures cryptography.

#5037. eToro’s Q3 profit plunges 74% as weaker crypto trading weighs on earnings

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

October crypto trades also fell 62% year over year to 2 million, while the trading platform announced a $250 million share buyback program.

Crypto and stock trading platform eToro (ETOR) reported a 74% year-over-year decline in third-quarter net income to $15 million, as weaker crypto trading weighed on its earnings.

The Nasdaq-listed company's net contribution fell 13% to $186 million from $215 million a year earlier, primarily due to reduced crypto trading activity, the firm said in its latest earnings release on Tuesday.

Adjusted EBITDA fell 56% to $34 million from $78 million in the same quarter last year. Revenue from crypto assets dropped to $1.35 billion from $3.97 billion a year earlier, and the associated cost of crypto asset revenue was $1.29 billion, compared with $3.89 billion for the same period in 2025.

In August, The Block reported that eToro had posted $53 million in second-quarter net income on $229 million in net contribution. Both figures were down from its record first quarter, although net contribution grew year over year on stronger equities trading. However, its first-quarter results had already shown a 50% year-over-year drop in crypto trades.

Beyond the third quarter, eToro's October figures showed crypto trades falling 62% year over year to 2 million. The average invested amount per crypto trade also declined 29% to $228 during the month, the firm said on Tuesday. Trading activity was also lower across equities, commodities, and currencies, with 48.2 million trades recorded during the month, down 22% from October 2025.

The platform ended September with 4.39 million funded accounts, an 18% increase from a year earlier, and assets under administration were unchanged at $20.8 billion. eToro's board also approved a $250 million share buyback program, with purchases subject to market conditions and the company's liquidity requirements.

#5034. Many Women Over 40 Avoid Jumping Workouts—But This Simple Exercise Is the Key to Stronger Bones

And you only need a small dose of plyo to reap the benefits.

THREE YEARS AGO, at 52, Tina Tang could deadlift 310 pounds without blinking and squat 210 like it was part of her warm-up. She had discovered powerlifting in her early 40s, and by all appearances, she was the picture of strength.

So when her annual physical revealed osteopenia—low bone density—she was stunned.

As a coach who trains women over 40, Tang believed she was doing everything right for her bones: lifting heavy, paying close attention to her form, eating enough protein to support muscle maintenance and growth. But one critical piece of the bone-health puzzle was missing from her routine, she would come to figure out: plyometrics.

Many women, particularly those over 40, have been told (or assume) that jumping is off-limits as they age. “The misconception is fueled by claims of increased joint pain, pelvic floor leakage, and the idea that bones become too fragile to tolerate impact,” says board-certified orthopedic surgeon, Pamela Mehta, MD, founder of Resilience Orthopedics in Los Gatos, California.

The truth: For most otherwise healthy women, plyometrics—and jumping and weight-bearing impact exercises more broadly—are among the most effective ways to protect bone health long term, says Dr. Mehta. And beyond just helping prevent bone loss, plyometric training can actually help reverse declines, emerging data suggests.

A recent review published in Current Osteoporosis Reports found plyometrics are among the most effective tools for improving bone density and reducing fracture risk in post-menopausal women. Meanwhile, a 2025 review in BMC Musculoskeletal Disorders found that when already-active people added plyometrics to their routine, they saw promising improvements in bone mineral density at the spine and hip.

Tang is living proof of these findings. At 54 years old, and just two years after adding plyometrics to her workout routine, Tang got a follow-up DEXA scan (the gold-standard test for assessing bone density) that revealed measurable improvements, including in her right hip, the area that had taken the biggest hit, per her initial scan.

“The trend is for women to continuously lose more and more bone as they age,” says Tang. Once you develop osteoporosis or osteopenia, only a small percentage of people can regain density. “So the fact that I improved mine—and plyometrics was the main thing I changed—is major,” she says.

So even though plyometrics may have gotten a bad rap among the 40-plus crowd, that reputation is rooted in nothing more than outdated pseudoscience and fear-mongering. These explosive, higher-impact exercises deliver the exact type of loading that spurs bone growth, development, and increased density.

In sum, stronger bones, better balance, faster reaction time, and a body that’s more resilient as you age are just a skip, hop, and (broad) jump away. Here's what to know—and what to do.

“Plyometrics are a category of exercises that require you to produce a tremendous amount of force in a short amount of time,” says strength and conditioning specialist Alena Luciani, MS, CSCS, founder of Training2xl. Often described as explosive or powerful, plyometrics are more impactful than traditional weight-bearing or high-impact exercises by design.

Consider traditional squats versus plyometric variations. Sure, goblet squats—which involve holding a weight in the front rack position as you sit to depth—can support bone density by challenging the musculoskeletal system, explains Luciani. But their higher-impact cousin, the jump squat, takes the stimulus to the next level. “You have to move faster and produce force much more quickly than you would during a goblet squat,” she says.

That rapid, high-impact loading is the exact type of stimulus that bones need to remodel and grow stronger, says Parini A. Patel, DO, an interventional pain and musculoskeletal medicine physician at Atlantic Health in New Jersey. In fact, recent research suggests that high(er) impact, more explosive training regimes (like plyometrics) provide even more bone benefits than traditional strength training.

One 2023 review published in the Journal of Sports Sciences observed that high-impact jumping exercise improved bone mineral density more than non-jumping alternatives.

Bones respond best when forces hit them quickly and sharply—like the impact of landing from a jump, explains Dr. Patel. “Plyometrics naturally provide these conditions in ways that low-impact activities cannot,” says Dr. Patel. Each time you land, sensor cells in the bones (called osteocytes) detect that burst of impact and signal the bone’s builder cells (osteoblasts) to lay down new bone, she explains. Over time, even short doses of this kind of impact can significantly improve bone strength.

Just as important as their impact on bone density, plyometrics also strengthen the systems that help prevent fractures in the first place. “Plyometrics also train and prime your central nervous system, helping to increase reaction time,” says Luciani. “Fast reaction time is significant for adults as they get older because it can be the difference between tripping, falling, and breaking a hip while walking on the sidewalk, and catching yourself so that the break never happens,” she says. This is key as hip fractures have been shown to increase the risk of future fractures drastically and are associated with higher mortality rates—especially in older women.

While plyo training has benefits for people across the gender spectrum, women are especially poised to reap the benefits. Women face significantly higher lifetime risks of osteopenia and osteoporosis by a large margin. (Eighty percent of all people with osteoporosis are women, studies suggest.)

One cause of this discrepancy? Hormones, says Dr. Mehta. Estrogen in particular has a protective impact on the bones; while the hormone is best known for its role in reproduction, estrogen supports new bone formation.

Unfortunately, that means that when estrogen levels drop during perimenopause and the menopause transition, the protective impact wanes and women start to lose bone mass—and quickly, says Dr. Mehta. (Men experience hormonal changes with age, too, but the effect of reduced testosterone levels on bone is far less dramatic.)

“Women also reach a lower peak bone mass in early adulthood compared to men, providing less skeletal reserve as age-related bone loss begins,” says Dr. Patel. In addition, women have smaller frames than men, and therefore are predisposed to have smaller bones, thinner cortices, and lower bone volume than men, she adds. With that, the natural declines associated with age and hormonal changes have more detrimental impacts on women over time.

Not to mention, because women live five to six years longer than men on average, “they spend more years in a lowest estrogen state," she says, which means the internal structure of the bones gradually weakens over time, raising the risk of fractures.

Women’s comparatively heightened risk for osteopenia and osteoporosis, broadly speaking, makes them ideal candidates for nearly any kind of bone-protective interventions. But plyometrics may be an especially powerful addition.

Consider this: The spine is the most common osteoporotic fracture site amongst women, while the hip is a close second. Breaks in either area can cause pain, as well as lead to immobility and loss of independence, which leads to other complications long-term. The good news is that both regions are highly responsive to plyometric training. “The hips and spine respond especially well because they absorb the most force when you land,” Dr. Patel says.

Importantly, health care providers emphasize that plyometrics aren’t meant to replace traditional strength training, but to complement it. Strength training supports muscle and tissue health more broadly, while plyometrics deliver the impact bones respond to particularly well, explains Dr. Patel. Together, the two types of loading work together to support the health of your entire frame—and research backs it up.

A study published in Applied Sciences last year found that participants who tacked on twice-weekly jump training to their usual exercise routine had improved lower-limb joint health in just 12 weeks. Meanwhile, a 2025 review in Current Osteoporosis Reports found that high-impact plyometric exercise increased lumbar spine bone mineral density in post-menopausal women. But, the authors note that the greatest benefits often appear in programs that combine resistance training with impact loading, reinforcing the idea that the two approaches work best together.

And those widespread myths that say plyometrics are off-limits after middle age? Hogwash. “Jumping does not cause wear-and-tear, worsen osteoarthritis, or increase fracture risk,” says Dr. Patel. “When done appropriately, plyometric drills are one of the most effective ways to stimulate bone formation and preserve bone density during midlife,” she says. The real risk arises from improper progression, inadequate landing mechanics, or unrecognized musculoskeletal conditions—not the jumping itself, she says.

No matter your age, goals, or training background, it’s wise to consult a physician before giving your movement practice a major overhaul, says Dr. Patel. A clinician can review your medical history, medications, bone density status, and any underlying conditions to determine whether impact training is appropriate for you at this time. Or, if you’ll need modifications.

If you have symptoms of pelvic floor dysfunction (e.g., urinary leakage, heaviness or pressure in the pelvis, pain during penetration, or discomfort during high-impact activities) it’s worth consulting a pelvic floor physical therapist first. Conditions like prolapse or incontinence often require some pelvic floor strengthening and coordination work before plyometrics feel safe and supportive, Dr. Patel says.

Once you’ve gotten the green light, the next step is to gradually introduce plyometrics into your workouts. For most, Luciani recommends picking just one or two plyometric movements per session, performing 8 to 12 total reps of each movement, with a minimum of 20 to 30 seconds between reps.

Perform these movements after your warm-up, but before your main strength work, when your nervous system is primed but still fresh enough to generate power safely.

That may not sound like much, but it’s the ideal dose. “You want to keep the volume really low because these movements are incredibly taxing on your tissues and nervous system,” she says. “The goal is quality, not quantity.” A smaller number of well-executed reps is far more effective (and safer!) than dozens of sloppy reps.

Workout Rx: Pick just one or two plyometric movements per session, performing 8–12 total reps of each movement, with a minimum of 20–30 seconds between reps.

1. Prep your body. Your current strength, balance, bone density, and overall fitness level should dictate exactly which plyometrics you introduce first. But Dr. Patel recommends beginning with movements that build Achilles tendon strength and lower-leg resilience, since the Achilles absorbs a large portion of the force when you land from a jump. Calf raises, heel drops, and small pogo hops can all help prep the tissues and joints for higher-impact training, she says.

As you hop, make sure to prioritize two-foot takeoffs and proper landing mechanics (soft knees, send back hips, engaged core), says Dr. Patel. This will help distribute impact throughout the lower body and reduce undue stress on your joints, all while still delivering the bone-building stimulus that plyometrics are known for, she explains.

Stay here for a minimum of two to three weeks, or as long as it takes for the hops to feel smooth and you to feel confident.

2. Level up to staggered-stance jumps. When you feel stable and controlled with two-foot hops, Tang recommends graduating to staggered-stance jumps. “Start with one foot just slightly in front—the change in stance forces your body to find its center of mass differently,” she explains. The mid-line strength and increased balance you gain here will make later progressions—like broad jumps and single-leg hops—much more accessible and safer. Just don’t forget to practice with both your dominant and non-dominant foot forward, as you want to build balanced strength and stability, she says.

3. Add more explosive options. After a few weeks of symptom-free, beginner-friendly plyometrics, you’re ready to try advanced plyometric exercises, such as:

These all require you to generate even more force even more quickly, which is precisely what makes them so effective for bone health—but also why they need to be approached thoughtfully, says Luciani.

5. Try unilateral plyometrics. “One of the hardest next levels is single-leg hopping,” says Tang. Unilateral plyometrics demand significantly more balance, stability, and force production from each leg—which is exactly why they’re so effective, she says. “Try hopping on a single leg in place, hopping forward, hopping left and right.” As you gain proficiency in these one-sided movements, try increasing the distance you cover with each hop,” she suggests.

6. Don’t forget about midline and upper-body plyometrics. Lower-body plyometrics may get all the attention, but if you want stronger bones or midline, you shouldn’t snooze on upper-body variations. “Upper-body plyometrics are fun to do, dynamic, great for stress relief, and often utilize a medicine ball,” says Luciani. These exercises—which include med-ball throws, d-ball slams, rotational wall throws, and the like—help you turn, rotate, react, and produce power through your upper and middle body, which is a real asset as we age,” she says.

Luciani suggests one of your two to three weekly plyometric sessions, an upper-body or midline-focused workout. Ideally, hitting them before an upper-body-focused lifting day, like shoulder and biceps or back and triceps.

7. Prioritize recovery. No matter your progression, plyometrics require a lot from your body. Yep, even though you’re only supposed to do fewer than 12 reps per session. “You want to allow 48 hours between your higher-intensity plyometric sessions for proper recovery,” says Dr. Patel. You can further support recovery by prioritizing sleep, eating nutrient-dense foods and enough of them, and implementing stress-relief exercises.

Additionally, be sure to check in with your body. “Sharp pain, new neurological symptoms, pelvic floor symptoms, and prolonged soreness are signs that it’s time to pause progression and check in with an expert,” she says.

#5033. Visa adds Monad to stablecoin settlement network options

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Payments company Visa has added the Monad blockchain network as an option to its stablecoin settlement program, allowing participating issuers and acquirers to settle through the network.

Monad joins a number of blockchains Visa already supports for the program, including Avalanche, Ethereum, Solana, Stellar, Circle's Arc, Base, Canton, Polygon and Tempo. Visa announced support for the latter five in April.

Blockchain settlement allows funds to move on weekends and holidays as well, compared with traditional card settlement, which runs on a five-business-day banking schedule, the Monad Foundation said.

"Our goal is to give our partners the flexibility to settle on the blockchain networks that best meet their needs," Dan Roesbery, Visa's vice president of global onchain partnerships and GTM, said in a statement. "Adding support for Monad expands the options available to our partners and is another step toward building a multi-chain settlement infrastructure for stablecoin-enabled payments."

Visa reported an annualized stablecoin settlement run rate of $20 billion as of September 2026, up more than 15x year over year. It also had more than 225 stablecoin-linked card programs live globally.

The feature is being built directly into Monad, so private transactions can use the same liquidity as other transactions on the network, the foundation said, adding that Monad can support this without slowing down settlement.

"The next phase will be defined by whether institutions can keep sensitive flows confidential without leaving open networks," Keone Hon, co-founder and general manager of the Monad Foundation, said in a statement.

Visa has also been exploring private settlement on Canton. In June, it announced a proof of concept with stablecoin issuer Brale to test settlement using its dollar-backed SBC token. The initiative focuses on how institutions can settle onchain while controlling who can see sensitive transaction data.

#5031. Moody’s Taps Chainlink to Send Credit Ratings Across Multiple Blockchains

Moody’s Taps Chainlink to Send Credit Ratings Across Multiple Blockchains

Chainlink and Moody’s Ratings have completed a pilot showing how credit ratings could be beamed across multiple blockchains at once, another sign that the traditional machinery of finance is steadily moving onchain.

Announced Tuesday, the test used Chainlink’s Cross-Chain Interoperability Protocol, or CCIP, to distribute Moody’s ratings simultaneously across both permissioned and permissionless blockchain networks.

It ran through the Moody’s Ratings Token Integration Engine, or TIE, the system the 125-year-old ratings giant built to disseminate credit assessments and related data within blockchain environments. The pilot, the companies said, preserved Moody’s existing publication and governance processes while proving the ratings could travel across chains through a single interoperable layer.

The logic is straightforward: as more bonds, funds, and other assets are issued as blockchain tokens, the investors trading them will expect the credit intelligence they rely on to be available wherever those assets live, rather than stopping at any one network’s edge.

Moody’s brings real experience here, having rated or assessed more than 70 digital issuances, including digital bonds and tokenized funds, between January 2017 and October 2026.

“As more financial assets move onchain, credit intelligence has to move with them, across chains and through every rating change,” said Johann Eid, chief business officer at Chainlink Labs, calling the pilot a foundational piece of what institutional capital needs to adopt tokenization at scale.

Fabian Astic, Moody’s global head of digital economy, argued that “the future of tokenized finance is unlikely to reside on a single blockchain, platform, or market infrastructure,” and that investors will increasingly expect ratings regardless of the underlying technology.

The effort builds on Moody’s steady push onchain. Earlier this year, the firm launched TIE on the institutional Canton Network before extending it to Solana, making its ratings machine-readable on a major public blockchain.

Chainlink, meanwhile, has positioned CCIP as the connective tissue for institutional tokenization, recently rolling out an upgraded version and reporting tens of billions of dollars in cross-chain value secured as banks and asset managers experiment with moving assets between networks.

The pilot, though, ran entirely on a testnet using representative test data, with no actual rating actions published onchain and no live dissemination involved.

It demonstrated technical capability rather than a launched product, but the companies said the model is designed to accommodate future rating updates under appropriate governance. For now, it’s a proof of concept, albeit one pointing toward a future where even credit ratings are just another piece of data flowing across blockchains.

#5030. Binance warns iOS flaw could expose crypto wallet keys

The exchange urged iPhone users to remove apps installed from untrusted sources, and it is unclear whether Apple’s latest iOS updates fix the flaw.

Update (Nov. 10, 2026, 12:14 pm UTC): This article has been updated to include a response from a Binance spokesperson.

Cryptocurrency exchange Binance warned Tuesday that an iOS flaw could expose crypto wallet recovery phrases and private keys if a user runs a malicious app on an affected iPhone.

Binance listed iOS 26.7.1, 27.0.1 and 27.2 among the affected versions that could allow malicious apps to read local files from other apps, putting seed phrases, private keys and login credentials “at risk across the entire ecosystem.” Binance added that this was “an iOS-level issue, not unique to Binance.”

Binance urged users to delete any apps installed from untrusted sources, update iOS and all apps to the latest official versions, and transfer funds to new wallets created on clean devices if they suspect a breach already occurred.

Blockchain security firm SlowMist flagged the issue earlier on Tuesday, saying a malicious app could read recovery phrases if a wallet stores them unencrypted on the device.

An iOS security researcher who goes by forcequitOS publicly disclosed the flaw on Sunday and credited another researcher who goes by 0xjohnny.

Apple released iOS 27.2.1 and 26.7.3 on Monday, but it remains unclear whether either update fixes the flaw. Cointelegraph contacted Apple about the vulnerability, but had not received a response by publication.

A spokesperson for Binance told Cointelegraph that the “underlying file-access risk may apply across the iOS ecosystem” but said it identified no confirmed cases where attackers used this bug to access cryptocurrency wallets or steal funds.

#5027. Bitdeer reports record Q3 revenue as net loss widens to $125.5 million

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The miner's net loss widened to $125.5 million despite higher mining output and AI Cloud revenue lifting quarterly sales to a record $320.7 million.

Bitdeer reported record third-quarter revenue of $320.7 million, up 89% from a year earlier, as the bitcoin miner expanded its mining operations and increased AI cloud revenue.

The Singapore-based miner's net loss widened to $125.5 million from $30.1 million in the same period last year, the company said in a statement on Tuesday. The loss included a non-cash $54.6 million charge tied to changes in the fair value of a digital-asset loan.

Bitdeer CFO Michael G. Potter said the company's adjusted EBITDA, which excludes several non-cash and other items, rose 173% to $69.6 million from $25.5 million.

Bitdeer mined 3,855 BTC in the quarter, up from 1,109 BTC a year earlier, as its total proprietary hashrate climbed to 86.4 EH/s from 35 EH/s, the company said.

Self-mining revenue rose to $217.5 million from $130.9 million, while co-mining contributed another $59.9 million. The company held just 47 BTC as of Sept. 30, down from 2,029 BTC a year earlier.

Bitdeer also reported $221.5 million in proceeds from digital-asset disposals during the quarter, primarily mined bitcoin, according to the statement. It ended September with $43.2 million in digital assets and $343.8 million in digital-asset receivables from a related party, bringing the combined balance to $387 million.

Meanwhile, AI cloud revenue increased to $20.4 million from $1.8 million a year earlier. The company reported an AI cloud revenue backlog of approximately $2.9 billion and said it had secured about 332 IT megawatts of AI cloud capacity.

"We are converting AI demand into long-duration contracts," Potter said. "Our AI Cloud revenue backlog has grown to approximately $2.9 billion, and construction at Tydal remains on target. Customer prepayments, together with project-level financing, are designed to fund AI capacity as it is contracted."

Bitdeer shares closed 9.9% higher at $6.61 on Monday, according to The Block's BTDR price page. The stock was down 2.7% in premarket trading on Tuesday.

#5024. Moody’s and Chainlink test cross-chain credit ratings distribution

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

This was a testnet demonstration using representative data, with no actual ratings published onchain or live dissemination processes involved.

Moody's Ratings and Chainlink completed a pilot demonstrating the cross-chain distribution of credit ratings across multiple blockchain networks using Chainlink's Cross-Chain Interoperability Protocol (CCIP).

The testnet pilot used Moody’s Token Integration Engine to simultaneously distribute credit insights across permissioned and permissionless networks, demonstrating how ratings information could be made available to tokenized assets across different blockchains while preserving Moody’s existing publication and governance processes.

Earlier this year, Moody's launched a token engine to bring its credit analysis onchain, starting with the Canton Network.

"The future of tokenized finance is unlikely to reside on a single blockchain, platform, or market infrastructure," said Fabian Astic, Global Head of Digital Economy at Moody's. "As digital markets continue to evolve, investors will increasingly expect access to credit ratings and associated information regardless of the underlying technology supporting an asset."

The release explicitly says this was a testnet demonstration using representative data, with no actual ratings published onchain or live dissemination processes involved.

"This pilot explored how interoperable infrastructure can help make credit ratings available across multiple blockchain networks while preserving the governance and publication processes that support confidence in our analytical opinions," Astic said.

Between January 2017 and October 2026, Moody's Ratings rated or assessed more than 70 digital issuances, including digital bonds and tokenized funds.

Of note, Chainlink recently expanded its collaboration with S&P Global Ratings to bring vault risk assessments onchain.

#5022. Anthropic Sets IPO Price Range at $70 to $78, Valuing Company at Up to $1.4 Trillion

Anthropic Sets IPO Price Range at $70 to $78, Valuing Company at Up to $1.4 Trillion

Anthropic has set a price range of $70 to $78 per share for its initial public offering, according to an updated filing with the Securities and Exchange Commission on Tuesday.

The AI company plans to sell 825 million shares of Class A common stock, 801.4 million of them new shares and 23.6 million from existing shareholders, on the Nasdaq under the ticker "ANT," which it revealed when it made its IPO filing public last month. At the top of the range, the offering would raise about $64.4 billion, including the selling shareholders' stake. Underwriters have an option to buy a further 123.75 million shares.

Anthropic expects to price the offering on November 18 and begin trading on November 19, and is targeting a retail allocation of about 20%, well above the norm for an IPO, CNBC reported, citing people familiar with the details.

Based on the roughly 13.09 billion shares the filing says will be outstanding after the offering, the range implies a market value of about $916 billion to $1.02 trillion. On a fully diluted basis, the company could be worth up to $1.405 trillion according to CNBC, up from the $965 billion valuation it announced in a funding round in May.

Alongside the IPO, seven of the companies that supply Anthropic's computing power have agreed to buy a combined $26 billion of Class N common stock, which has no voting rights, at the IPO price. At the midpoint of the range, Google and Nvidia would each buy about $10 billion, AMD about $2.5 billion, Broadcom, Micron and Microsoft about $1 billion each, and TSMC about $500 million, according to the filing. Those shares will not be subject to the usual post-IPO lock-up period.

Anthropic expects net proceeds of about $84.6 billion from the IPO and the private placement combined at the midpoint, which it plans to use for general corporate purposes, including investment in compute and infrastructure, according to the filing.

The company reported revenue of $33.2 billion for the first nine months of 2026, up from $2.5 billion a year earlier. It posted a net loss of $104 billion over the period, most of it from a $94.3 billion non-cash charge tied to the rising value of its convertible notes and other instruments; its operating loss was $1.26 billion. Anthropic has also committed to about $900.9 billion of spending, mostly on compute and infrastructure, of which about $70.7 billion is due within the next 12 months.

Anthropic will list as a public benefit corporation with an unusual governance structure. Its Long-Term Benefit Trust, a body of financially disinterested trustees, will elect a majority of its board, while a single share of Class F stock held by an entity owned by its seven co-founders will carry 50.1% of the company's voting power on most matters, according to the filing. As a result, Anthropic will be a "controlled company" under Nasdaq rules, and holders of its Class A shares will have limited influence over major decisions, the filing warns.

The range comes after a turbulent week for AI stocks. Some major investors told The Wall Street Journal last week that Anthropic's expected $1.5 trillion valuation would likely need to come down following a sell-off in AI stocks, with one suggesting a figure closer to $1 trillion. Anthropic had been eyeing a valuation of around $2 trillion as recently as September, according to an earlier draft filing.

The IPO's order book is already covered at the proposed range, CNBC reported, citing people familiar with the matter.

#5020. Coinbase, Kalshi join lawsuit backing CFTC over US stock perp futures

Coinbase, Kalshi and four other companies backed the CFTC in a lawsuit over whether US exchanges can list perpetual futures tied to individual stocks.

Coinbase, Kalshi and four other companies asked a federal court Monday to accept a brief backing the United States Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC) in a lawsuit over perpetual futures tied to individual US stocks.

According to a proposed amicus brief shared Monday by Coinbase legal representative Molly Abraham, the group also includes Kraken parent Payward, trading companies DRW Holdings and Jump Trading, and crypto investment company Paradigm Operations. The companies argue that stock perpetual futures fall under existing US futures laws.

The filing comes days after a federal judge found that the CFTC likely “exceeded its statutory authority” in allowing single-stock perpetuals to trade as security futures.

The case could determine whether US exchanges can offer retail traders contracts that track individual stocks without expiring.

On Nov. 3, the CFTC announced that single-stock perpetual futures, or stock perps, could begin trading on US-regulated exchanges for the first time.

The Capital Markets Alliance Association (CMAA), a newly formed trade and advocacy group representing traditional financial market participants and whose members include Cboe and Citadel Securities, sued the CFTC last week, challenging the CFTC’s authority to allow the contracts to trade.

Stock perps allow traders to speculate on the price movements of individual stocks without owning the shares. Unlike traditional futures, they have no fixed expiration date.

The CMAA argues that because the contracts lack a fixed expiration date, they should be regulated as security-based swaps, a type of derivative linked to stocks or other securities that is primarily overseen by the Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) and faces tighter restrictions on retail trading.

The six companies backing the CFTC disagree, arguing that a fixed expiration date is not required for a contract to qualify as a security future, which falls under joint CFTC and SEC oversight.

Related: Coinbase’s US national trust bank charter takes effect

Following the CMAA’s complaint, US District Judge Valerie Caproni said the plaintiff was likely to succeed in its claim that the CFTC exceeded its statutory authority by allowing stock perps to trade as security futures.

Caproni found that the contracts appeared to qualify as swaps rather than security futures, casting doubt on the agency’s legal interpretation.

However, she denied CMAA’s request for a temporary restraining order, finding that the group had failed to demonstrate imminent, irreparable harm. The decision left the CFTC’s trading approvals in place while the lawsuit proceeds.

CMAA is scheduled to file its reply to the CFTC’s opposition to a preliminary injunction on Tuesday.

#5018. MEGA commits $30 million to new products, starting with AI inference

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The MegaETH Foundation rebrands as MEGA and commits $30 million to new products, with an AI inference market set to launch in December.

The MegaETH Foundation, the team behind the Ethereum Layer 2 network MegaETH, has rebranded as MEGA and committed $30 million to new products, starting with an AI inference market planned for December.

The announcement comes after months of work in stealth mode, MEGA said Tuesday, adding that the team will now also develop new products alongside the MegaETH network.

All new products will run on MegaETH, MEGA said. Planned network upgrades, including fast finality, will also help support its AI inference product.

Inference is the process of running a trained AI model to produce an output. MEGA said providers charge different prices for similar services, but buyers may find it hard to know the risks of using cheaper providers they are not familiar with.

"There are still huge gaps between what providers charge for comparable inference," MegaETH co-founder Yilong Li said in a statement. "We think those gaps can be dramatically reduced."

MEGA calls its approach "Purified Intelligence," aiming to make inference from different providers more interchangeable, allowing more suppliers to compete in a global market.

Asked how the market would work and which providers or models would be available, MegaETH co-founder Shuyao Kong told The Block that MEGA had intentionally held back those details until the December launch.

"We believe crypto is the only technology that makes AI fair and open," she said, adding that developers will be the target customers for the inference market.

The $30 million will come from MEGA's existing treasury to fund a range of products, Kong said, noting inference as the project's "largest move."

MEGA said its mandate includes expanding the uses of the MEGA token. When asked what role the token would have in the inference market, Kong said: "This is our mandate, so wherever there are ways to utilize the MEGA token, we will."

The MegaETH team has raised more than $80 million to date across venture funding, community rounds and token sales. That includes a $20 million seed round announced in June 2024, led by Dragonfly with backing from Vitalik Buterin, Joseph Lubin and others; a $10 million Echo round in December 2024; about $13 million from its Fluffle NFT sale in February 2025; and about $40 million from its public token sale in October 2025, according to Kong. She said the public sale amount reflects discounts for buyers who agreed to lock their tokens for one year.

Kong also noted that MegaETH bought back some investors' stakes last year. As The Block previously reported, the team repurchased a 4.75% stake comprising equity and token warrants from pre-seed investors in summer 2025.

The MegaETH network launched its public mainnet earlier this year and currently has about $12 million in total value locked, down from its peak of over $245 million, according to DefiLlama data.

The MEGA token went live in April this year and is currently trading at around $0.05, down over 76% from its all-time high, according to The Block's MEGA price page.

#5015. Bitcoin may benefit as global tightening slows, Binance Research says

Binance Research says Bitcoin could find support as global monetary tightening slows, even as markets price in a December Fed rate hike.

Bitcoin could find support before major central banks start cutting rates if the spread of rate hikes continues to slow, Binance Research said Tuesday.

Eight of the 10 central banks Binance tracks had raised rates over the last six months, including Sweden’s announced increase on Nov. 4, with the momentum of tightening starting to ease since its July peak, Binance Research said in a Tuesday report shared with Cointelegraph.

“Whether this easing continues into early 2027 will be key to judging whether the macro backdrop is turning less hostile for crypto,” the report said.

Binance pointed to the previous cycle. Bitcoin (BTC) bottomed in November 2022, and its six-month return turned positive in February 2023, before any of the central banks in the index began cutting rates.

Pace of G10 banks tightening and Bitcoin’s 6-month returns. Source: Binance Research

Similarly, in 2024, monetary tightening measures led to Bitcoin delivering its “strongest” six-month return of the cycle, exceeding 160% in March, Binance said.

“In each case, BTC moved with the direction of global policy, not its level,” the report adds.

Related: Analysts see no broader ‘contagion’ from $6.3B Binance hack

A US rate increase remains in play. Federal Reserve Governor Christopher Waller said Tuesday he was leaning toward a quarter-point hike in December and would consider a half-point move if core inflation remained elevated. Odds of a 25-basis-point hike in December rose from 76.3% to 96.3% on Tuesday, according to CME Group’s FedWatch Tool.

In a Nov. 2 report, Bitwise also argued that a slowdown in economic growth expectations could eventually prompt central banks to pivot towards easing monetary policy, potentially creating a more favourable environment for Bitcoin.

BTC/USD, 1-year chart. Source: CoinMarketCap

Bitcoin’s price fell 3.5% during the past week, extending its one-year decline to 30%. The world’s first cryptocurrency was trading at $74,131, or 41% below its $126,198 all-time high, according to CoinMarketCap.

#5012. Canaan’s Q3 revenue plunges 90% to $14.5 million as bitcoin mining hardware sales slump

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Cryptocurrency and derivatives gains helped the company report an $8 million quarterly profit.

Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) mining hardware maker Canaan Canaan Inc. (CAN) sold 1 million TH/s of computing power in the third quarter, bringing in $4.8 million from mining equipment, down 96% from $118.6 million in the same period last year.

Canaan's overall revenue fell 90% to $14.5 million from $150.5 million a year earlier, according to its financial results published Tuesday. Canaan attributed the drop in hardware revenue to lower computing power sold and weaker average selling prices amid reduced market demand.

When Canaan reported its third-quarter 2025 results last November, it had sold more than 10 EH/s of mining machines and produced 267 BTC, The Block reported at the time. The company mined 133 BTC in the latest quarter, generating mining revenue of $9.5 million, compared to $30.6 million in Q3 2025.

Nevertheless, Canaan posted $8 million in net income for Q3 2026, compared with a $27.7 million loss a year earlier amid a $20.1 million fair-value gain on cryptocurrency holdings and another $31.9 million on financial derivatives. However, its operating loss widened to $42.9 million from $23.9 million.

Canaan Chief Financial Officer Jin "James" Cheng attributed the gains to bitcoin's higher price at the end of September. "We recognize that this non-cash gain is distinct from underlying operating performance," he said in the release.

Earlier this year, Canaan acquired Cipher Mining's 49% stake in three West Texas bitcoin mining projects for approximately $40 million. The three projects had a combined installed hashrate of 4.87 EH/s at the end of September, according to the firm.

ERCOT classified Canaan's proposed 1 GW Duval County power project as "Studied Load – Firm" on a conditional basis, and further studies and approvals are still pending. Its second 1 GW Texas project, in Zapata County, remains in early development, the firm said.

Of the approximately 1,875 BTC Canaan held at the end of September, 1,258 BTC was pledged for secured loans. The company also announced a $20 million share buyback program under a $100 million authorization approved by its board on Nov. 6.

#5011. What Running the New York City Marathon Topless After Breast Cancer Was Actually Like

Following her historic NYC Marathon run, Lauren Spiezia opens up to Women’s Health in an exclusive essay about the thing that surprised her the most.

On Sunday, I ran the 26.2 miles of the New York City Marathon completely topless to raise awareness for breast cancer and show people what survival really looks like. Despite all the training, the experience still surprised me.

My legs hurt. My quads cramped at mile 16. The wind chapped my chest. I thought about my form. I thought of my husband Elio, who was running beside me, and whether he was okay. I thought about how grateful I was to run with him and to have a family and support system waiting for me at the finish line and throughout the course. I thought about what a privilege it is to wake up and move my body. And I thought, repeatedly, about not stopping.

Before my run, I wrote that I wanted to do this so I could inspire other women to advocate for themselves and feel okay in their bodies. I ran with a QR code temporarily tattooed on my back that led to a free Tyrer-Cuzick breast cancer risk assessment in hopes that the attention could highlight one of the tools to help women understand their risk. When I finally crossed the finish line, my heart was so full. We had raised over $50,000 for cancer research, helping the very hospital where I had received my own treatment just one year prior.

What I was not thinking about was the fact that I was topless. That was the strangest part. Some people have wondered how I ran without a sports bra. The answer is that because my surgeries had severed the nerves in my chest, I didn’t feel anything. By the time I was at the starting line with the gun minutes away, that decision had already been made a year ago. All that was left was to run.

Mid-race, I passed a group of girls holding a sign that read “This is Women in Sports.” I locked eyes with them. They pointed at me, jumping up and down. Their sign was a nod to a recent sports betting ad that had people talking about women, sexuality, and the representation of female athletes. These girls were making their own statement. This—the marathon and its record 28,942 women running it—was women in sports. I almost backtracked against the current of runners to hug them, but the race carried me on.

There were, of course, plenty of jaws that dropped. That was expected. But I didn't anticipate how many people would cheer me on—from the women on the sidelines who put their hands on their chests in solidarity when I passed them, to fellow runners encouraging me as we ran side-by-side even though I could barely hear them over the crowd, to the volunteers who handed out water cups, telling me they had the same scars.

That was the biggest surprise. It wasn’t the hecklers or the attention, but the love from strangers on the best day in New York City. The response since has been overwhelming.

My inbox is full of women and men I’ve never met. Some are people in treatment. Some just finished treatment. Some whose mothers, daughters, sisters, and aunts were just diagnosed. Women I have never met who want to ask me whether they can see my reconstruction so they can make the decision for themselves. The answer is always yes.

I have also received the opposite. Comments calling me inappropriate. Comments about my size, my choice to reconstruct, my decision to use implants. Comments that I was doing this for attention or for the camera.

Most of the shaming has come after, not during. On the course, people either cheered or stared. But online, people typed.

I made a conscious decision to read them. Not because they do not sting. They do. But because I know that the women I most want to reach—the ones sitting in a chemo chair, the ones who just got a biopsy result, the ones bracing to look at their chests in their bathroom mirror for the first time—are also reading the comments on my page right now. If they see me stay quiet or disappear, they will learn that is what women with bodies like ours should do.

To the people shaming me, I would say this. When you shame a woman’s body online or in person, you do more than silence her. You keep us from talking clinically about our bodies. You keep us from making informed decisions. You keep us from going to the doctor. You keep us from advocating for ourselves. You keep us quiet.

Breast cancer does not care about your opinion on my implants. It does not care whether you think my reconstruction counts. Breast cancer comes for 1 in 8 U.S. women in their lifetimes, and can affect men too. If breast cancer does one day impact someone you love, you will have wanted the women who went through this before them to have spoken loudly.

In 2024, Louise Butcher ran the London Marathon topless with her own mastectomy scars uncovered. She paved the way for women like me. After I first had the idea of running the New York City Marathon topless, I reached out to her to ask about her experience. We spoke again after marathon day. She checked in on the race and on my mental health. She understood, in a way almost no one else can, what the commentary around this kind of run can do to a person. Her message to me was simple. There will always be noise. There will always be criticism. Remember your why.

Louise and I also both noted how outlets have either blurred or strategically covered my chest. A mastectomy removes the breast tissue, and in my case, it removed the nipples too. Nothing on my chest needs censoring or blurring. There is just my chest—a body with prosthetics. Any blurring suggests there is something explicit worth hiding, when there isn’t.

There is also a thread of criticism that suggests some reconstruction choices are more “legitimate” than others. Louise chose flat closure. I chose implants. Neither of us is right or wrong. These are deeply personal choices, and every woman deserves the space to make hers without being audited for authenticity.

I understand the marathon is a family event and that not everyone will agree with the choice I made. Cancer happens to families too. But I hope my run started some important conversations.

Every photo and video I have seen of myself from race day shows the same thing. A young woman who built herself back up, brick by brick. I’m smiling and full of joy. The light that cancer had dimmed in me is now back.

If my run reminded even one woman to go for her annual check, to investigate a suspicious lump, to advocate for herself when a doctor dismisses her, the run was worth it. The scrutiny does not change that.

#5009. Solana's Jupiter Expands Beyond Trading With Token Baskets, Yield Vaults and New Portfolio

Solana's Jupiter Expands Beyond Trading With Token Baskets, Yield Vaults and New Portfolio

Jupiter, the largest trading platform on Solana, is rolling out four new products this week as it aims to expand from a place to trade tokens into an all-in-one home for onchain assets.

The company announced a new mission on Tuesday, "to make the world's assets universally accessible and useful," alongside the releases, which cover discovering, owning, earning on and managing assets.

Jupiter built its business by aggregating liquidity from across Solana so that each trade gets the best available price. It says that approach has processed more than $1.3 trillion in cumulative aggregator volume, and that its wider ecosystem now holds about $1.7 billion in total value locked, according to DeFiLlama data from early November. Its lending market has passed $1 billion in active loans, the company said.

"What Google did for information, Jupiter is doing for assets," said Kash Dhanda, Jupiter's chief operating officer. "We started with the trade because it was the hardest problem on Solana, and aggregation solved it. That taught us something: aggregation doesn't just work for trading, it works for everything."

Dhanda has framed the goal in even bigger terms. "I'm a big believer that the era of do-it-yourself DeFi is over," he said in a livestreamed interview with Incrypted on October 29, recalling having "12 tabs open and a spreadsheet" to manage positions across different venues. He said he hopes Jupiter "in five years' time will be the only money app that you need to use."

Baskets let users buy a bundle of tokens with a single SOL or USDC deposit instead of making a separate swap for each one. Baskets can follow market-cap weights or fixed targets and rebalance automatically, with every deposit, rebalance and exit routed through Jupiter for the best available price.

Jupiter Vaults is a new hub for yield strategies, from stablecoin lending to strategies backed by real-world assets, managed by independent curators rather than Jupiter itself. Users can review each strategy's risk profile, allocation and historical returns before depositing. Vaults opens on November 11 as a private beta for more than 19,000 people on its waitlist and eligible holders of staked JUP.

In the October interview, Dhanda said Jupiter's yield products are audited multiple times before reaching users. Asked what would happen if a product were exploited, he said that "especially if it's our fault, we'd like to work with users to find a good solution," and that Jupiter is exploring ways to insure such products onchain.

Portfolio V3, live now, turns Jupiter's portfolio page into a place to act on positions, with quick actions to trade, open perpetual futures, make predictions or lend, and alerts for positions near liquidation. It also adds cross-chain transfers that combine several bridges.

Pulse and rebuilt token pages are meant to make Jupiter a place to research assets as well as trade them, showing market data for tokenized stocks and ETFs, tokenomics for DeFi projects, and a sidebar for comparing tokens. Dhanda has argued that putting assets onchain is only half the job. "People talk about tokenization as if it is the end game, when in reality, it is just the first step of the game," he said in October.

The releases will roll out over the coming days, with Jupiter sharing details of each one before Solana Breakpoint, the network's annual conference, which starts on November 15.

All Vaults strategies carry risk, including the possible loss of funds, and some may not be available in all regions, including the United States, Jupiter said.

Jupiter's launches come amid a flurry of activity across the Solana ecosystem ahead of the conference. Last week, Solana Labs' new company Leap launched a beta mobile app for trading tokenized stocks, ETFs, metals, currencies and crypto in one place.

The network has also been courting institutions. Last month, the Solana Foundation unveiled an open standard for institutional settlement developed with input from J.P. Morgan, while Polygon Labs extended its Open Money Stack, a suite of stablecoin payment tools linking bank accounts, wallets and payouts, to Solana.

Under the hood, Solana completed its biggest consensus upgrade since its 2020 launch in October, when Alpenglow went live and cut the time for a transaction to become irreversible from about 12.8 seconds to roughly 150 milliseconds. Solana treasury firm DeFi Development Corp has also added $3 million worth of SOL to its holdings.

Jupiter powers token swaps on The Information Exchange, Decrypt's trading platform.
